## 🆕 `p775` — **Foundations for the exclusion and suspension register (`ACTION p774-I`). 🟢 `7` permissive foundational rows, every grant graded on the bytes this pass against a canonical set grown from `27` to `36` texts. 🔴 And two platforms this base has carried for passes are corrected: `sakaiproject/sakai` is NOT altered, and `openeducat/openeducat_erp` ships a TRUNCATED LGPL-3.0 that is 19% of the canonical document.**

### 🟢 `9` canonical licence texts added this pass (`ACTION p774-B` — discharged, with a measured payoff and a measured limit)

🔵 `ingest/canonical/` grows **`27` → `36`**. Added, all fetched from the SPDX licence-list text set on the raw channel and byte-checked: `BUSL-1.1` (`3,424 B`), `BSL-1.0` (`1,338 B`), `ECL-1.0` (`2,425 B`), `EUPL-1.0` (`13,005 B`), `OSL-3.0` (`10,319 B`), `AFL-3.0` (`10,334 B`), `CECILL-2.1` (`21,778 B`), `CC-BY-NC-4.0` (`17,649 B`), `CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0` (`19,018 B`).

🟢 **Payoff, measured:** `openstax/osbooks-college-physics-bundle` resolves to **CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0** at `0.9893` and `sdv-dev/SDV` to **BUSL-1.1** at `0.8688`. Neither was resolvable before this pass.

🔴 **Limit, measured, and it is the more useful half:** on `ACTION p774-B`'s **named test set** — the `8` `NOT-IN-CANONICAL-SET` rows — the widened set resolved **`2` of `8`** (both `rrule` forks, to altered BSD-3-Clause) and left `6`. Widening the canonical set is **not** the instrument for that bucket, because a bespoke restrictive licence is by construction far from every open-source text. **`NOT-IN-CANONICAL-SET` is where non-free licences hide; low similarity is a positive signal, not a null result** (`T780`).

🔴 **And `BSL-1.1` is not an SPDX identifier at all.** SPDX has `BSL-1.0` — the **Boost** licence, permissive — and `BUSL-1.1`, the Business Source licence, which is **not open source**. Both texts are now held specifically so the collision is mechanically detectable: a one-character slip between them inverts the commercial verdict (`T781`).

## 🆕 `p774` — **Foundations for the admissions and appeals register (`ACTION p773-I`). 🟢 `6` permissive foundational rows — the first register in nine where the FOUNDATION of the mechanism is MIT rather than absent. 🔵 **Why these six and not the usual LMS list:** an admissions deliverable is not a content system. ## 🆕 `p772` — **The foundations shelf re-graded on the body-diff, not re-stocked (`ACTION p771-C`). `23` SPDX canonical licence texts are now held in `ingest/canonical/`, which is the standing instrument `p771-C` asked for.**

### 🟢 `ingest/canonical/` — the held canonical set, and why SPDX rather than a shelved payload

`ACTION p771-C` required: *"hold a canonical payload per family in `ingest/`."* `p771` had used a repository payload graded canonical by inspection. This pass holds **SPDX's own `license-list-data`** instead — the authority that defines the identifiers these shelves publish.

| Family | Canonical bytes | `sha256₁₂` |
|---|---|---|
| `Apache-2.0` | `10,280` | `074e6e32c86a` |
| `MIT` | `1,078` | `b05785f9f18e` |
| `BSD-3-Clause` | `1,460` | `5a93d5831e12` |
| `BSD-2-Clause` | `1,267` | `f32fb3b417a1` |
| `GPL-3.0` | `34,674` | `fb981668c18a` |
| `AGPL-3.0` | `34,020` | `d8a6cc31abc1` |
| `LGPL-3.0` | `42,098` | `996af0513df2` |
| `MPL-2.0` | `16,727` | `66a3107d5ad6` |
| `ISC` | `737` | `521c6f0ed8e6` |
| `Unlicense` | `1,211` | `0bdebfeda07d` |
| `Zlib` | `838` | `bfb1112d49db` |
| plus `GPL-2.0`, `LGPL-2.1`, `CC-BY-4.0`, `CC-BY-SA-4.0`, `CC0-1.0`, `EPL-2.0`, `Apache-1.1`, `BSD-4-Clause` and four `-or-later` variants | — | — |

🔵 **Note the canonical `Apache-2.0` is `10,280 B`, not the `11,357 B` this base has called canonical for many passes.** Both are correct and they are different things: `11,357 B` is the **shipped `LICENSE` file** (terms **plus** the "how to apply" appendix); `10,280 B` is SPDX's **licence text**. 🟢 **The body-diff compares operative text only — everything up to `END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS` — so the two reconcile, and `p771`'s `11,357 B` census remains valid as a file-length fingerprint.**

🔵 **`5` of the `35` are Brazil's federal VLibras sign-language stack (`spbgovbr-vlibras`), all at `7,650`–`7,651 B`** — the LGPL-3.0 *addendum only*, without the GPL-3.0 text it incorporates by reference. 🟢 **That is not a defect in the repository: LGPL-3.0 is defined as GPL-3.0 plus additional permissions, and shipping only the addendum is a common and arguably correct reading.** 🔴 **It is a defect in any instrument that grades a grant by length or by hash** — and this base ran a length rule for several passes (`p769-B`, falsified at `p770`). **`T747` is why that rule kept failing on copyleft.**

🟢 **For a LATAM accessibility engagement this is directly useful: VLibras is federal Brazilian government open source, consistently licensed, and the stub payload is a reading of LGPL rather than a missing grant.**

### 🔴 `T748` — `42` addresses publish a licence claim with no grant file at the root

A licence family is asserted on the shelf, and **`10` grant filenames** (`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENSE.md`, `COPYING`, `LICENCE`, `LICENSE.rst`, `license`, `COPYING.txt`, `LICENSE-MIT`, `LICENSE-APACHE`) all miss at the measured default branch.

🔵 **`AccessKit/accesskit` was in this set before it was graded, and it is the instructive case: the grant exists, under two filenames the probe list does cover, and the *root* `LICENSE` does not exist.** 🔴 **So `NO-GRANT-PAYLOAD` must be read as "not at the root under a known name", never as "unlicensed"** — the `T691`/`NHTung` class of error in reverse (`ACTION p772-C`).

### 🟢 Foundations supply, stated plainly

🔴 **No repository was added to this shelf this pass, and none was removed.** A sweep pass verifies; it does not stock. The `12` foundation rows `p771` verified stand, each now carrying a body-diff verdict in `evidence/p772-licence-verdicts.tsv`.

# Foundational Repositories — Education

### 🔵 `T724` — the legacy `rrule` citation resolves, so it is harmless

`ACTION p770-B` prescribed the right test: *does the DISPLAYED ADDRESS RESOLVE?* Applied to this base's long-standing `jkbrzt/rrule` citation: **`jkbrzt/rrule` and `jakubroztocil/rrule` both return `HEAD` `9f2061febeeb63d03352efe33d30c33073a0242`** — the same repository by owner redirect. 🟢 **Harmless, same class as `NREL/*`.** 🔵 The canonical form is published above because it is the one that will still resolve if the redirect is ever dropped.

## 🆕 `p770` — **The foundation layer of the school-catering register is a product-level allergen corpus at global scale, and the one state-published platform on it handles the PROCUREMENT duty and contains `0` allergen tokens. `6` foundation rows byte-read over complete trees, `1` licence payload proven to be a dangling pointer, and the length channel this base was told to trust at `p769-B` shown to be degenerate.**

Every grant located by **complete-tree enumeration** and read from the cloned blob with `wc -c` and `sha256`; every branch from `git ls-remote --symref`. **No path guessed, no licence taken from an index.**

### 🟢 The foundation rows

### 🔴 A fifth by-reference form for `ACTION p769-D`, and this one points at a file that is not there

`p769-D` listed four by-reference grant forms: DEP-5, the CRAN field stub, the Sphinx `.. include::` and Eigen's prose licence map. **`OpenEduCat`'s root `LICENSE` is a fifth, and structurally unlike all four.**

```
For copyright information, please see the COPYRIGHT file.

OpenEduCat is published under the GNU LESSER GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE, Version 3
(LGPLv3), as included below. Since the LGPL is a set of additional
permissions on top of the GPL, the text of the GPL is included at the
bottom as well.
```

🟢 **It is a POINTER PLUS PAYLOAD**: the first line delegates ownership to another file, and the remaining `8,200`-odd bytes are the full LGPL-3.0 and GPL-3.0 texts. The other four forms are pointer-*only*, so a classifier that treats `byref:` and `grant:` as exclusive grades this wrongly either way.

🔴 **And the pointer dangles: there is NO `COPYRIGHT` file anywhere in the `1,345`-file tree.** 🟢 **So the grant is unambiguous and the ownership is unobtainable from the repository** — a sharper case than `769-G`'s `Insert your license here`, where the path existed and the grant did not. Here the grant exists and the named ownership record does not (`T723`, `ACTION p770-D`).

### 🔴 The length channel is degenerate, which disposes of `ACTION p769-B` as written

`p769-B` told this base to grade every published grant `ownership-readable` or `ownership-blind` by diffing its byte length against the canonical census. **Both halves of the rule fail on this register's payloads.**

| Test | `p769-B` predicts | Measured | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| MIT at `1,064 B` (`allergengrid`), inside the canonical band | `ownership-blind` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2026 KanadeK`** | 🔴 **False negative** |
| MIT at `1,069 B` (`ma-cantine`), inside the canonical band | `ownership-blind` | 🔴 **`Copyright (c) 2022 beta.gouv.fr`** | 🔴 **False negative** |
| MIT at `1,182 B` (`openfoodfacts-python`), deviating | `ownership-readable` | 🟢 `Copyright (c) 2016 OpenFoodFacts, Inc.` | 🟢 **Held** |
| Two AGPL payloads at `34,523 B` each | same payload | 🔴 **`8486a10c4393` vs `0d96a4ff68ad`** | 🔴 **The channel itself is degenerate** |

🟢 **Mechanism for the fourth row, diffed rather than inferred:** the payloads differ on `2` lines of the AGPL's "how to apply" appendix, where the word `by` sits at the end of one line in one copy and the start of the next in the other. **One space trades places with one newline: the length is preserved and the hash is not.**

🟢 **Corrected rule, which keeps the half that works:** a deviating length is a *sufficient* signal that a payload **may** be read for ownership; a canonical length is **not** evidence that it may not, because a short owner name leaves the length canonical. **Fingerprint on the hash. Establish ownership by reading the copyright line.** (`T721`.)

### 🔴 `ACTION p769-E` — the second licensing question, asked for the first time and answered empty

`p769-E` directed this pass to start asking what grant the **distributed artefact** carries, not only the source: *"for every row whose deliverable is a shipped binary, enumerate `*license*.in`, `installer/`, `packaging/` and `debian/` for a distribution-time grant."*

🟢 **Executed over all `11` enumerated trees on this register. Result: `0` distribution-time grants, and the reason is structural rather than reassuring** — not one row on this register ships a built installer. They are a Django service (`ma-cantine`), two client libraries, a Python CLI, a React component, a PHP app and an Odoo module set. 🔵 **The question is now in the enumerator and costs nothing to keep asking; this register simply does not exercise it.** 🔴 **`EnergyPlus`'s CMake-templated EULA (`9,407 B`) remains the only instance this base holds, and it is still unresolved for a redistributing studio** (`ACTION p770-E`).

### 🟢 `ACTION p768-B` corroborated for a copyleft family for the first time

The prohibition — never prove identity, a redirect or ownership with a canonical licence payload — has been evidenced on MIT and Apache-2.0 templates across five repositories. 🟢 **This pass adds AGPL-3.0:** `34,523 B` · `8486a10c4393` is byte-identical on [`MustrHQ/kitchen-safety`](https://github.com/MustrHQ/kitchen-safety) (a UK kitchen-temperature log) and on `ladybug-tools/honeybee-energy` as recorded at `769-B` **on an unrelated register** — and `34,523 B` · `0d96a4ff68ad` is byte-identical on [`openfoodfacts/openfoodfacts-server`](https://github.com/openfoodfacts/openfoodfacts-server) and [`theKaido/Kaleidogram`](https://github.com/theKaido/Kaleidogram). 🔵 **Two distinct AGPL variants, each shared by two unrelated projects, at one byte length.**

|---|---|
| [`betagouv/ma-cantine`](https://github.com/betagouv/ma-cantine) | 🔴 `staging` |
| [`openfoodfacts/openfoodfacts-python`](https://github.com/openfoodfacts/openfoodfacts-python) | 🔴 `develop` |

🔴 **`4` of `11` trees have a default branch that is neither `main` nor `master`, and one of them is a release number.** 🟢 **`T713`'s instruction to take the branch from `--symref` rather than assume it is upheld, and `19.0` is a new shape: a grant probe that iterates `main`, `master`, `develop` still misses it.**

### 🔴 The exposure analysis the root grant hides — and the remedy the upstream publishes

`EnergyPlus`'s root is **BSD-3-Clause**. Enumerating the complete tree returns **`27` grant paths across `9` distinct families**, and three of them are copyleft:

| Path | Family, byte-read | What it actually is |
|---|---|---|
| `third_party/eigen/COPYING.GPL` | 🔴 **GPL-3.0** · `35,147 B` · `8ceb4b9ee5ad` | Eigen's legacy licence *option* |
| `third_party/eigen/COPYING.LGPL` | 🔴 **LGPL-2.1** · `26,530 B` · `dc626520dcd5` | Eigen's legacy licence *option* |
| `third_party/eigen/bench/btl/COPYING` | 🔴 **GPL-2.0** · `18,109 B` · `b8d0e2bcbf36` | A benchmark harness, not built into the product |
| `third_party/eigen/COPYING.MPL2` | 🟢 **MPL-2.0** · `16,726 B` · `fab3dd6bdab2` | **Eigen's actual primary grant** |

🟢 **And `third_party/eigen/COPYING.README` is an upstream-published prose licence map that ships its own remedy:**

```
Eigen is primarily MPL2 licensed. See COPYING.MPL2 ...
Some files contain third-party code under BSD or LGPL licenses ...
If you want to guarantee that the Eigen code that you are #including is licensed
under the MPL2 and possibly more permissive licenses (like BSD), #define this
preprocessor symbol:  EIGEN_MPL2_ONLY
```

🟢 **So the copyleft exposure in the register's foundational engine is real, enumerable, and removable with one compile flag that causes a build error if any LGPL header is included.** 🔵 **This is the first time on this base that a copyleft finding came with a mechanical, checkable remedy rather than an architectural quarantine.** `P353` carries it as a build-gate assertion rather than a sentence in a report (`T709`).

🔴 **But the classifier correctly refuses `COPYING.README` itself** — it is a prose map with no operative clause. **A fourth by-reference form, and unlike DEP-5 it is not machine-parseable** (`ACTION p769-D`).

### 🔴 Two grant paths that contain no grant, and a third that is the wrong grant

| Finding | Payload | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **An unfilled template is not a licence** | `OpenStudio` carries **three** `LICENSE.md` files whose entire content is `Insert your license here` — `24 B` · `0d980364ebe9`, in BCL measure templates | 🔴 **A probe that asks *"does a `LICENSE` path exist?"* counts all three as licensed.** Stronger cousin of `ACTION p768-B`: there a canonical payload carried no *ownership*; here the payload carries no *grant at all* (`769-D8`, `T710`) |
| 🔴 **The source grant is not the distribution grant** | `EnergyPlus` carries `cmake/license.in.txt` — `9,407 B`, an **END USER LICENSE AGREEMENT** with `@CMAKE_VERSION_MAJOR@` substitution placeholders | 🔴 **A CMake-templated EULA that ships with the *binary installer*.** The repository is BSD-3-Clause for the source; the artefact a client installs carries a EULA. **For a studio that redistributes a built binary these are two different questions, and this base has only ever asked the first** (`T711`, `ACTION p769-E`) |
| 🟢 **A permissive family with no name in its own text** | `third_party/penumbra/vendor/glfw/LICENSE.md` — `904 B` · `149704059b5d` | 🟢 **Zlib.** The payload never states the licence's name, so a family-name matcher cannot see it and it graded `UNRECOGNISED`; the discriminator must be the *as-is warranty plus altered-source-marking* clause pair (`769-D7`) |

### 🟢 `ACTION p768-B` corroborated a third time — and its positive complement stated

`11,357 B` · `c71d239df917` appeared **three times this pass**, on a Chinese commercial carbon platform, a US hobby RL wrapper and a Chinese EMS gateway. 🟢 **`evidence/apache-baseline-pair.2026-10-27-p486.tsv` records this fingerprint as the GitHub licence-picker template**, and `p768` proved it byte-identical across a Japanese ministry repository and an abandoned German hobby project. **The prohibition stands and is now evidenced on five unrelated repositories.**

🟢 **The complement, which `p768-B` did not state: a NON-canonical length may carry ownership, and here one does.** BBSR's MIT payload is `1,153 B` — not the `1,063`/`1,069`/`1,070`-ish canonical MIT lengths this base has censused — because the copyright line was **filled in**: `Copyright 2026 Bundesinstitut für Bau-, Stadt- und Raumforschung (BBSR) im Bundesamt für Bauwesen und Raumordnung (BBR)`. 🟢 **Procedure: a grant payload's deviation from the canonical length is the signal that it may be read for ownership; a match to the canonical length is the signal that it may not** (`T712`, `ACTION p769-B`).

Every grant located by **complete-tree enumeration** and read with `cat-file blob`; no path guessed, no licence taken from the index.

Pushed `2026-11-05` |

### 🟢 The pass's instrument find: `ACTION p766-D`'s requirement was already solved, and this base was discarding the solution

`ACTION p766-D` required a classifier that *"admits more than one family per repository, scoped by path"*, because Metabase is AGPL outside `enterprise/` and commercial inside it. `p767-D` then recorded `debian/copyright` as **noise**, on the grounds that `95` of QGIS's `126` matched paths were `cpt-city` colour-gradient metadata.

🟢 **`debian/copyright` in DEP-5 form is not noise. It is exactly the artefact `p766-D` specified, published by upstream as data.** From `roidelapluie/readesm`:

```
Format: http://www.debian.org/doc/copyright-format/1.0
Files: *
License: GPL-3+
Files: ressources/translations/readesm_de.ts
Copyright: 2009,2010,2011 Martin Walch and Andreas Gölzer
```

It is a **per-file-glob licence map** with a stable, parseable grammar. 🔴 **It declares the licence by reference and substitutes a literal `[LICENSE TEXT]` placeholder, so a clause-text classifier correctly refuses it — and therefore silently discards the best evidence in the repository.** `ingest/p768-grant-classifier.py` now parses it and marks every result `byref:` so no reader mistakes a declaration for a read payload (`T696`, `ACTION p768-D`).

### 🔵 Apache-2.0 at three distinct lengths in one pass, and the baseline is this base's own

| Length | `sha256₁₂` | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `11,358 B` | `cfc7749b96f6` | 🟢 **apache.org's authoritative text** — `traccar`. Matches `p486`'s `AUTHORITATIVE canonical` row exactly |
| `11,357 B` | `c71d239df917` | 🔵 **The GitHub licence-picker template**, one leading blank line fewer — `Project-PLATEAU`, `sebastian-tschen/fahrtenschreiber`. Matches `p486`'s second fingerprint exactly |
| `11,328 B` | `9ebd66b7d7dd` | `google/transit` — a third, shorter variant |

🟢 **`p765`'s `T646` census is corroborated from a fourth direction**, and three MIT lengths repeated inside this pass alone (`1,063 B` and `1,069 B` each carrying **two distinct payloads**). 🔴 **The consequence is `T692`: a canonical grant `sha256₁₂` identifies the licence text, never the repository.**


## 🆕 `p767` — **`11` foundation rows, every grant path **enumerated from the full tree** instead of guessed. The pass's structural result: a repository does not have *a* licence, it has a **map from path scope to grant** — and `qgis/QGIS` proves it at a scale that settles `ACTION p766-D(ii)` nine times over, where `p766`'s Metabase case proved it twice.**

**`5` geospatial substrate repositories + `6` register and field-capture repositories · `45,540` tree paths swept for the substrate alone · every `default_branch` from `git ls-remote --symref` · every family from **operative clause text**, never the index and never the licence's own name.**

### 🔴 `ACTION p766-D(ii)` discharged — and "one licence per repository" is not an approximation, it is wrong

`p766-D` asked the detector to *"admit more than one family per repository, scoped by path,"* citing Metabase's AGPL-outside / commercial-inside-`enterprise/` split. Enumerating whole trees shows that split is ordinary:

| Repository | Grant paths in tree | Distinct families | The reading a root-only probe publishes |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`qgis/QGIS`](https://github.com/qgis/QGIS) | **`126`** | 🔴 **`9`** — GPL-2.0, GPL-3.0, LGPL-3.0, Apache-2.0, MIT, ISC, BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, SIL-OFL-1.1 | `GPL-2.0`, and nothing about the `LGPL-3.0` declared in `debian/copyright` |
| [`postgis/postgis`](https://github.com/postgis/postgis) | **`11`** | **`5`** — GPL-2.0, Apache-2.0, ISC, BSD-3-Clause | 🔴 **`COPYING` *and* `LICENSE.TXT` are BOTH root grants** (`18,092 B` · `8177f9751321`; `3,580 B` · `c6932c2f9051`). A probe that stops at its first hit reports one of two |
| [`OS4ED/openSIS-Classic`](https://github.com/OS4ED/openSIS-Classic) | `20` | `5` | 🔴 **nothing — there is no root grant.** The project grant is **GPL-2.0 at `docs/License.txt`** (`17,286 B` · `224ac3d403e3`) |
| [`stiles/la-trees`](https://github.com/stiles/la-trees) | `49` | `3` | `MIT` — and not that `node_modules/` is committed |
| [`tyrasd/Baumkataster-Graz`](https://github.com/tyrasd/Baumkataster-Graz) | `2` | `2` | `MIT` — and not the **BSD-3-Clause** icon set at `icons/LICENSE.txt` |

🟢 **The rule this base should carry forward:** a **root** grant governs the repository; a **non-root, non-vendored** grant governs its own subtree and is a *project* statement (openSIS, QGIS's `debian/copyright`); a non-root grant under `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `third_party/`, `libraries/` or `assets/` is a **vendored third party** that a fork inherits. `ingest/p767-grant-classifier.py` emits all three distinctions per path (`T683`).

🔴 **And the enumerator's precision cost, measured rather than hidden:** of QGIS's `126` matched paths, **`95` are `cpt-city` colour-gradient metadata files named `COPYING.xml`** — not grants at all. The classifier correctly refuses them (`UNRECOGNISED`), so the pipeline is *safe but noisy*: **a path named like a grant is not a grant, and only a byte-read settles it.** `2` more are Creative Commons **3.0** statements in prose, for which the family table — carrying only 4.0 — has no entry (`ACTION p767-D`).

## 🆕 `p766` — **The register has almost no tooling but the *substrate* is permissive and complete, and this pass found the one primitive eight previous patterns said did not exist: a statutory interval is an **RFC 5545 recurrence rule**, and the two libraries that implement it are BSD-3 and Apache-2.0. `3` licence-index errors corrected by byte-read, one of them a licence reachable only from a README.**

**`12` permissive and weak-copyleft foundation rows, each naming the branch its grant was read from.** Register: **playground-equipment inspection** (`ACTION p765-E`). Every grant below was byte-read on `2026-11-11` from the `default_branch` returned by `search/repositories`; no branch was guessed and no family was taken from GitHub's licence index.

### 🟢 `ACTION p765-F` is dischargeable, and here is the part that was missing

`P326`–`P332` each said the same thing: *nothing in open source models a statutory duty as `{duty, instrument, interval, competent person, evidence type}`*. That was true of the **platforms** and it was never true of the **primitive**. A statutory interval — *routine, operational, annual main* — is a recurrence rule, and recurrence rules have had a stable standard since 1998 (RFC 5545 / RFC 2445) and two mature permissive implementations. The engine is now committed as data at [`evidence/statutory-intervals.2026-11-11-p766.tsv`](../evidence/statutory-intervals.2026-11-11-p766.tsv), with an `interval_rrule` column that drops straight into either library (`T661`, `P334`).

## 🆕 `p765` — **The substrate for a school statutory-compliance system is permissive almost all the way down — `25` MIT payloads and `11` Apache-2.0 payloads byte-read — and the three components that are *not* usable are the three that encode the law: the specification is NoDerivatives, the standards body took its code private, and the one artefact that knows schools exist is NonCommercial.**

**`27` permissive and weak-copyleft foundation rows, each naming the branch its grant was read from.** Register: **school-estate safety and statutory compliance** — fire doors, asbestos, legionella, electrical fixed-wire testing, playground-equipment inspection (`ACTION p764-A`). The register's technical content is three layers: **field capture** (an inspection happens away from a desk), **the asset model** (the thing inspected has to be identifiable), and **the interval engine** (a statutory duty is a date, not a state).

**`61` licence payloads byte-read with length and `sha256₁₂` · `32` licence-family paths per address including capitalisation of stem **and extension** · `16` language manifests on failure · `5` `COPYING.LESSER`-family paths on any GPL title · every family confirmed by **operative clause text**, never by byte count (`ingest/p765-grant-classifier.py`) · `54` new addresses and `18` re-reads, marked per row.**

### 🟢 Field capture — the layer an inspection actually runs on, and it is Apache-2.0 without exception

🟢 **`ACTION p761-E` closes here, with a mechanism.** `getodk`'s `NOASSERTION` has been unexplained since it was raised and `p764` explicitly declined to claim a cause. The cause is that the Apache text is **reformatted as Markdown** at `LICENSE.md` — a setext underline, italics on the version line, HTML-escaped angle brackets — so the index, which matches canonical text, declines (`T633`).

### 🔴 Named absences, so the silence is not read as coverage

- **`11` addresses published as absences** after `32` licence paths and `16` manifests each: `Batool-Jaber/ifc-compliance-checker`, `Gariknik/FireCodeCheckerAgent`, `mightyoung/young-agent-app`, `phoenixgit321/ai-property-safety-inspection`, `itmicus/cmdbuild_docker`, `chowtao123-design/shopfloor-cmms`, `Ramki-K/asbestos-material-detection`, `ryjord/Argus`, `kerwinarlan/dorm-evacuation-signage`, `Safety-Bin/ehs-system`, `rhyspaul192/AsTrak`. 🟢 **A twelfth was refuted** — `TowerScout/TowerScout` at `LICENSE.TXT` (`T630`), which is why `ACTION p765-A` now requires a README read before an absence is published.
- 🔴 **No interval engine exists.** A statutory duty is a recurring date with a competent-person attribute and an evidence artefact. Nothing on this shelf models that; every candidate models a *work order*, which is a task without a legal deadline. **This is the gap a Globant deliverable fills, and it is small enough to build.**
- 🔴 **`topic:asbestos` returns `5` repositories worldwide** and `topic:legionella` returns `12`, against `topic:compliance`'s `10,822` — which is the software-compliance register, not this one (`T629`).
- 🔴 **No permissive artefact encodes a statutory interval table for any jurisdiction.** The intervals exist in England, India and three US states (`T642`–`T645`); none is in code under any licence this base could find.

## 🆕 `p764` — **The permissive substrate for the school estate exists, is maintained, and was built by a national energy laboratory whose GitHub organisation renamed itself out from under every citation in the sector. `18` permissive rows, every grant byte-read. And a `25,442★` MIT building editor that ships an MCP server is the single best starting point this base has ever filed for a physical-asset register.**

**`18` permissive and weak-copyleft foundation rows, each naming the branch its grant was read from.** The register is the school estate — buildings, work orders, the deferred-maintenance backlog, the utility bill and the capital programme. 🟢 **The measuring and modelling layer is permissive end to end, again** (`T622`). 🔴 **The layer that *operates* the estate is copyleft or source-available without exception, and is named separately in `verticals/solutions.md`.**

### 🔴 The discovery finding: `NREL` is gone, and it took `788` repositories with it

`repo:NREL/EnergyPlus` returns **`422 Validation Failed`** — *"the listed users and repositories cannot be searched"*. So does `org:NREL`. The repository is not deleted: `energyplus in:name` returns **[`NatLabRockies/EnergyPlus`](https://github.com/NatLabRockies/EnergyPlus)** at `1,622★`, pushed `2026-11-11`. **The organisation renamed from `NREL` to `NatLabRockies` and it now holds `788` repositories.**

🔴 **The rename is visible in the grant bytes, not just the address.** `OpenStudio`'s payload reads *"Copyright (c) 2008-2026, **Alliance for Energy Innovation, LLC**"* and `SAM`'s reads the same, while `ComStock`'s still reads *"Copyright (c) 2025 **Alliance for Sustainable Energy, LLC**"* — two different named licensors inside one organisation on the same day. **The lab's operating entity changed name and the estate is mid-migration.** Any `NREL/...` citation in any knowledge base is now a dead address at the API.

🔴 **And the channel `ACTION p756-A` proposed for exactly this case is unavailable here.** That action directed the next pass to *"probe the GitHub HTML endpoint, which does redirect"*. Measured this pass: `https://github.com/NREL/EnergyPlus` → **`403`**; `https://github.com/CEDStandards/CEDS-IDS`, a live address this base verified yesterday → **`403`**; `https://github.com/Grashjs/cmms` → **`403`**. The HTML endpoint is blocked at this tunnel for *every* address, live or renamed, so it cannot distinguish them. 🟢 **The working rename detector is the pair `422` on `repo:` + a hit on `<name> in:name`** (`T621`).

### 🟢 `p757`'s law, fifth consecutive register — and its first partial counter-example

Permissive, byte-read today: `pascalorg/editor`, Home Assistant, all four `NatLabRockies` engines, Brick, both VOLTTRONs, Indico, A11yJSON, bSDD, two CMMS implementations, NUcore, trier-os, respa. Copyleft or source-available: every platform that *runs* an estate (`verticals/solutions.md`).

🔵 **The partial counter-example is `liberusoftware/project-maintenance-laravel-filament` and `SuperCMMS/Open-Source-CMMS`**: two MIT systems of record for maintenance, at `73★` and `44★`. The law should now be read as *"what holds the record at scale is copyleft"* — the permissive record-holders exist, they are just `20×` smaller than the AGPL-3.0 one.

### 🔴 Declared absences on this shelf

- **`openMAINT` — widely described as the most complete open-source maintenance management system — has no canonical repository on GitHub.** `openmaint in:name,description` → `22` results, every one a Docker wrapper or a client; the highest-starred is [`itmicus/cmdbuild_docker`](https://github.com/itmicus/cmdbuild_docker) at `125★` with **no grant file**. The upstream lives on its own site under its own distribution terms. 🔵 **Not an absence of software — an absence of the supply channel this base can measure.**
- **No open-source condition-assessment tool.** Not one repository implements a facility condition index, a condition grade, or a lifecycle-replacement model — the three things every one of the four regional statutory instruments demands.

## 🆕 `p763` — **The reference implementation of the US statewide longitudinal data system is Apache-2.0, actively maintained, and completely unfindable: the programme's own name returns `0` repositories. The permissive substrate for this register is `14` Apache-2.0 repositories across two standards bodies — and a third distinct sha256 at exactly `11,357` bytes proves again that no size gate can identify a licence.**

**`24` permissive foundation rows, all byte-read, every row naming the branch it was read from.** The register is the education data warehouse and the statutory return. 🟢 **The whole measuring layer is permissive end to end.** 🔴 **The layer that holds the student record is copyleft without a counter-example and is named separately in `verticals/solutions.md`** so a studio does not reach for it by accident.

### 🔴 The discovery finding of the pass: the buyer's own vocabulary returns nothing

`statewide longitudinal data system` → **`total_count 0`** on the repository-search channel. The programme behind that phrase is funded at `$28.5M` a year and has reached `50` states, `5` territories and DC across eight rounds. **The reference implementation is `CEDStandards/CEDS-IDS` — "Integrated Data Store" — and nothing in its name, description or topics contains the buyer's phrase** (`T604`).

### 🔴 `T587` — a grant one hop from the probed path, and a pointer file that looks like a grant

`valhalla/valhalla` serves `master/LICENSE.md` with a `200`, so every existence and licence probe this base runs terminates successfully. The payload is **`44` bytes**:

```
For license information see [here](COPYING)
```

A size-banding gate reads `44 B` as *not MIT, not Apache, not GPL* and files the row as unknown or as a licence absence. The actual grant is **MIT** at `master/COPYING`, one path away. 🔵 **A `200` is not a grant. A probe must classify the payload, and a payload under ~`200 B` is a pointer to be followed, not a verdict** (`ACTION p761-D`, below).

### 🔴 `T589` — this pass's own honest-negative verdict was wrong twice, and both times on the biggest repository in the batch

This pass's licence script probed `8` filenames × `3` branches = `81` paths per address and returned `10` verdicts of `ALIVE-NO-GRANT`. The follow-up refuted **`2`**:

| Repo | ★ | The verdict | The truth, and why the probe missed it |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`Project-OSRM/osrm-backend`](https://github.com/Project-OSRM/osrm-backend) | `8,209` | 🔴 "no grant at `81` probed paths" | 🟢 **BSD-2-Clause at `master/LICENSE.TXT`.** The path set carried `LICENSE.txt` and `LICENSE.md` but **not the uppercase extension** `LICENSE.TXT` |
| [`frappe/erpnext`](https://github.com/frappe/erpnext) | 🟢 **`41,172`** | 🔴 "no grant at `81` probed paths" | 🟢 **GPL-3.0** · `35,149 B` · sha256₁₂ `3972dc9744f6` at **`develop/license.txt`** — lowercase filename, **and on a branch that is neither `main` nor `master`**. Two independent misses compounding |

🔴 **That is a `20%` false-negative rate on this batch, and it is not randomly distributed: both false verdicts landed on the two highest-starred repositories in the `NO-PAYLOAD` set.** Large, old projects are exactly the ones with idiosyncratic licence filenames and non-standard default branches, so the honest-negative class `T302` is **biased against the repositories a client is most likely to name**.

🟢 **`ACTION p761-D` — three fixes, all mechanical, all testable:**
1. Add `LICENSE.TXT`, `LICENCE.TXT`, `license.txt`, `license.md`, `COPYING`, `COPYING.md` to the probe path set.
2. **Derive the branch — do not guess it.** `search/repositories` returns `default_branch` on every item; this pass read it to settle `T588` and could have read it for every row. Guessing `{main, master, develop}` found `erpnext` only by luck.
3. Follow any payload under `200 B` as a pointer (`T587`).
4. **Re-probe the accumulated `T302` honest negatives above ~`1,000★` under the corrected path set.** A published licence absence on a large repository is now a measured-unreliable claim.

### 🔵 What this shelf measures, stated as a ratio

🔴 **`5` copyleft, `5` permissive — and the `5` permissive rows resolve to `3` organisations, of which one holds the three that matter.** 🔵 **This is a concentration finding, not a licence finding.** The mitigation is not legal: it is to budget for owning the Kolibri-chain integration and to carry Learning Equality's roadmap as a named project risk in the proposal (`T570`, `P295`).

## 🆕 `p759` — 🟢 **THE WHOLE SAFETY-AND-RECORDS SUBSTRATE FOR A DISCLOSURE-TRIAGE PIPELINE IS MIT / APACHE-2.0 / BSD-3 / CC0. 🔴 THE ONE COMPONENT AT `3,200★` THAT EVERY CLIENT WILL PROPOSE IS ARCHIVED, AND TWO GRANTS ARE INVISIBLE TO GITHUB'S INDEX.**

**`9` foundation rows, every grant byte-read `2026-11-11`** at ref `HEAD` with length and sha256₁₂ over `raw.githubusercontent.com`. 🟢 **The `★` column is measured on the fetch channel per `ACTION p757-E`** — no cell on this shelf says `unmeasured`. 🔴 **`4 / 9` publish from a default branch that is not `main`** (`master` ×`3`, `develop` ×`1`), reconfirming `Gap 263` on a sixth estate (`T563`). 🔴 **`2 / 9` grants are not named by GitHub's index at all, from two different mechanisms** (`T560`).

🔵 **Why these are foundations and not agents.** None of them reasons about a student. They are the deterministic layer a wellbeing or safeguarding agent must be wrapped in: **de-identify** before any model call, **constrain** what the model is allowed to say, **record** the case and the referral in a real clinical format, and **measure** the whole thing well enough to hand an auditor. 🔴 **On this register the wrapper is not optional and it is not polish — it is the deliverable.** The model is the cheap part.

| [`microsoft/presidio`](https://github.com/microsoft/presidio) | 🟢 **`MIT`** — `1,083 B`, sha256₁₂ `ffe37ea6fa0b` · holder *Presidio Contributors* · *index agrees* | `11,800` | 🔵 **Global** — corporate holder with no region in the payload | 🟢 **De-identification, and it is work package one rather than a hardening step.** Detects, redacts, masks and anonymises PII across text, images and structured data, with both NLP and pattern matching. 🔴 **A safeguarding disclosure is the most identifying text in a school's estate** — it names a child, often a family member, often an alleged perpetrator — and `p750` recorded the US federal position that holding such records apart from the education record **itself infringes FERPA**. 🟢 **So the redaction boundary is a records-architecture decision with a regulator's attention on it**, and this is the permissive instrument for it. `main`. |

| [`NVIDIA/NeMo-Guardrails`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/NeMo-Guardrails) | 🟢 **`Apache-2.0`** — but by **identifier, not text**: `LICENSE.md` is **`653 B`**, sha256₁₂ `e08f3ab8c83a`, carrying `SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` plus the boilerplate notice · 🔴 ***index is SILENT*** — GitHub names no licence | `7,500` | 🔵 **Global** — holder *NVIDIA CORPORATION & AFFILIATES* | 🟢 **Programmable dialogue rails, and the live replacement for the archived row below.** Lets a conversation be constrained by rules rather than by prompt text: refuse a topic, force an escalation path, never emit a diagnosis. 🔴 **This is the component that makes `T549` deliverable** — a hard rail that the system cannot be talked out of. 🔴 **Two defects worth carrying: the licence is invisible to the index (`T560`), and it publishes from `develop`**, so a `main`-pinned tool reads nothing and the grant at `HEAD` is the unreleased one (`T563`). |

EMNLP 2024. 🟢 **A public-domain dedication — fine-tune on it *and* ship the result**, no attribution, no downstream restriction. 🔴 **Direct counter-example to `p758`'s `T538`** (`CC BY-NC-ND` ASR corpus: neither tunable nor shippable). ⚠️ **Chinese only** (`T562`). `main`. |

| 🔴 [`protectai/llm-guard`](https://github.com/protectai/llm-guard) | 🟢 `MIT` — `1,093 B`, sha256₁₂ `1ff4e6c23a28` · holder *Protect AI* · *index agrees* | `3,200` | 🔵 **Global** | 🔴 **FILED AS A WARNING: THE REPOSITORY IS ARCHIVED AND READ-ONLY.** *"The Security Toolkit for LLM Interactions"* — input/output scanning for prompt injection, PII leakage and toxicity. 🔴 **It is the single most plausible wrong proposal on this register**: `3,200★`, `MIT`, exactly the right shape, and no longer maintained — which on a pipeline that children talk to is a security posture, not an inconvenience. 🟢 **Use `NeMo-Guardrails` + `guardrails` + `presidio` above instead.** 🔵 **Archive status is invisible in a licence and in a star count and must be read per address, every pass** (`T557`). |

## 🆕 `p758` — 🟢 **THE CAPTIONING SUBSTRATE IS MIT/APACHE/BSD ALL THE WAY DOWN AND THE ESTATE RUNS ON IT ALREADY. 🔴 BUT THE ONE COMPONENT THAT PROVES CONFORMANCE WAS NEVER ON A SHELF, AND THE BEST TIMESTAMP TOOL IS ARCHIVED.**

🔴 **One foundation-shaped component is missing and its absence is now measured, not assumed.** Forced alignment of a *pre-existing* transcript to media — the cheapest path to conformance when an institution already has lecture notes or a script — has exactly one mature open implementation, [`readbeyond/aeneas`](https://github.com/readbeyond/aeneas) (`2,876★`), and it is 🔴 **`AGPL-3.0`** (`34,519 B`, sha256₁₂ `91b65277959e`, byte-read). It is therefore **not** a foundation row on this shelf: under Globant's delivery model an AGPL component in a hosted pipeline reaches the client's own obligations. It is filed on [`verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) as a warning instead (`T546`).

## 🆕 `p757` — 🟢 **THE RESEARCH-ADMINISTRATION SUBSTRATE IS PERMISSIVE ALL THE WAY DOWN — `MIT`, `Apache-2.0` AND `BSD-3-Clause` — AND `756` PASSES HELD `0` OF IT. 🔴 AND ORCID'S OWN CODEBASE IS NOT `MIT`.**

**`10` foundation rows, every grant byte-read `2026-11-11`** with length and sha256₁₂ recorded over `raw.githubusercontent.com` at ref `HEAD`. 🟢 **The `★` column is populated again — `p756` wrote `unmeasured (403)` into every cell of this shelf on the strength of a dead `/repos/` path, while the live `search/repositories` path answers the same question (`T521`).**

🔴 **The rule for this shelf, and it is the inverse of `p756`'s.** `p756`'s rows were an *assessment* substrate whose licences were mostly stock and whose risk was concentrated in two outliers. **This register's risk is concentrated in its identity layer**: the three registries every scholarly workflow resolves against — ORCID for people, ROR for organisations, Crossref for works — are permissive in two cases and **bespoke in the one that matters most**.

[`skillab-project`](https://github.com/skillab-project) is an EU-funded (Horizon) consortium shipping the full pipeline for this register: a curriculum-skills crawler, an auto job-ads collector, an occupational-demand forecaster, an explainable occupation analyser and a tracker. `10` repositories visible, **`13 / 13` licensed artefacts carry `EPL-2.0`**.

🔴 **Read this before proposing the Skillab stack, because `EPL-2.0` is the licence most often mis-summarised in this base's subject area.** It is **not** permissive and it is **not** GPL. Its copyleft is **file-scoped**: modifications to EPL-2.0 files must be released under EPL-2.0, but a larger work that merely *links* the component may stay proprietary. 🔴 **It also carries a patent-retaliation clause and a defensive-litigation termination**, and — the clause that actually bites in a services engagement — **a distributor who offers the software commercially must indemnify the other contributors** against liability arising from that commercial offering. 🟢 **Usable in a Globant engagement, but it is a legal review, not a dependency bump**, and it is categorically a different conversation from the MIT and Apache-2.0 rows above it.

### 🔴 `T450` — the licence seam in this register, and it is the opposite of the one `p750` found

`p750` recorded a seam where *measuring* was permissive and *holding the record* was copyleft. **This register's seam runs between the general and the vertical:**

- 🟢 **General-purpose solvers: `13 / 13` permissive** — Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD-3, BSD-2 or MPL-2.0. Not one copyleft grant among them.
- 🔴 **Education-specific solving: copyleft wherever it exists** — `UniTime/cpsolver` **LGPL-3.0**, `yuroyami/FET-MCP` **AGPL-3.0**, `karandit/fet` indexed **AGPL-3.0**, FET itself **GPL-family**.

🟢 **The commercial reading, in one line:** *the moment someone writes the education semantics down, the result has historically been copyleft — which means the permissive education scheduler is an unoccupied position, and Globant can occupy it by building on `or-tools` or `timefold-solver` rather than by extending `cpsolver` or FET.*


**Pass p741 · verified 2026-11-10.** Building blocks rather than deployable platforms:
assessment engines, learner-model implementations, exam corpora and evaluation benchmarks.
Deployable systems (LMS, ERP, conferencing) are on `verticals/solutions.md`.

Stars, SPDX licence and `pushed_at` read this pass via `gh api -X GET search/repositories`
(**alive at `p736`; `p735`'s death notice is retracted — see `T310`**); existence confirmed on the
raw channel; **every licence verdict below decided by reading the payload, not the index** (`P188`). Where the search index has no entry for a
repository that demonstrably exists, that is stated in the row rather than treated as absence.

> 🔴 **`p727` added no row to this page, and that is a measured nil rather than a skipped leg.**
> The mandated foundational leg (`open source platform {industry} ERP CRM MIT Apache`) returned
> `openeducat/openeducat_erp`, `frappe/lms`, `OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`, `francoisjacquet/rosariosis`
> and `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` — **`5 / 5` already held on this page or on
> `verticals/solutions.md`.** The `grading agent LLM rubric` phrase leg returned **zero rows at
> all**. `p727`'s three finds were all *agents* and are filed on `agents/top.md`.
> 🟢 **What this page gained instead is four re-verifications, three of which contradict the
> search index and none of which contradict this base:** `openeducat_erp` indexes
> `NOASSERTION` and is **LGPL-3.0** by payload (`LICENSE`, `8,241 B`, read `2026-11-09` — the
> vendor's own LGPL claim is correct); `rasbt/LLMs-from-scratch` indexes `NOASSERTION` and is
> **Apache-2.0** (`11,505 B`, matching the `p687` cell); `OpenOLAT` indexes `NOASSERTION` and is
> **Apache-2.0** (`10,982 B`, matching `p676`); `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` returns **`404` on all
> four root licence paths**, reproducing the standing finding that its terms exist only at
> `docs/License.txt` (`GPL-2.0`) where no root-filename probe can reach them.
> 🔵 **Three of four index fields are wrong; `4 / 4` of this base's payload-derived cells are
> right.** Recorded as the clearest evidence yet that reading the bytes is worth its cost.


## 🆕 `p750` (2026-11-10) — 🟢 **THE WELLBEING SENSING AND RECORD-STORE LAYER: `6` PERMISSIVE ROWS FROM A TIER THIS BASE HELD AT `0`. 🟢 THE LICENCE GRADIENT CONFIRMS `T360`'S LAYER SEAM A FIFTH TIME, AND THIS TIME THE SEAM RUNS BETWEEN *MEASURING A STUDENT* AND *HOLDING A RECORD ABOUT THEM* — `7 / 7` PERMISSIVE AGAINST `4 / 4` COPYLEFT, NO CROSSOVERS. 🔴 ONE ADDRESS THE LITERATURE CITES IS `422`, AND ITS SUCCESSOR IS AT A DIFFERENT ORGANISATION.**

### 🟢 `T427` — the seam, measured on a fifth estate, and the architectural rule falls straight out again

| Layer | Rows | Licences observed | Crossovers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sensing, instrumentation and measurement** — what observes or asks | `7` | BSD-3-Clause ×`5`, Apache-2.0 ×`2` | **`0`** |
| **The record about the person** — case files, disclosures, clinical notes | `4` | AGPL-3.0 ×`3`, CPAL-1.0 ×`1` | **`0`** |

🟢 **The rule, now stated as a general law of this base after five independent confirmations
(`T360`, `T410`, `T415`-adjacent, `T423`, `T427`):** *the layer that **measures or checks** is
permissively licensed; the layer that **holds the person's record** is copyleft.* 🔵 It is not a
coincidence — a case-record system is deployed by the institution that needs it and shared with peer
institutions, which is exactly the economics AGPL protects, while an instrument library wants adoption.
🔴 **The consequence for a studio is the same every time:** own the sensing and scoring layer, call the
record layer across a process boundary, and never link it (`P253`).

🔵 **Why Beiwe and not a fitness-tracker SDK.** Beiwe is the Harvard/Onnela-lab digital-phenotyping
platform: it was built to be the *measurement instrument of a published study*, so consent, study
configuration and data provenance are first-class rather than bolted on. 🟢 **That is exactly the
property that makes it survivable in an institution where the alternative — commercial student
monitoring — is being sued** (see [`../intel/market.md`](../intel/market.md), North America).

## 🆕 `p749` (2026-11-10) — 🟢 **THE ALTERNATIVE-FORMAT ESTATE: `10` PERMISSIVE ROWS FROM A TIER THIS BASE HELD AT `0` MENTIONS. 🔴 THE LICENCE GRADIENT RUNS ALONG THE *LAYER* AND IT IS CLEAN: EVERY CHECKER AND EVERY FORMAT SPEC IS PERMISSIVE, EVERY ASSISTIVE-TECHNOLOGY RUNTIME IS COPYLEFT, `8 / 8`, NO CROSSOVERS. 🔴 TWO `daisy` REPOSITORIES HAVE NO GRANT FILE AT ALL.**

### 🟢 `T423` — the gradient, measured on the whole tier, and the architectural rule falls straight out

This confirms `T360`'s layer seam a **fourth** time, on an estate no prior pass had touched, and this
time with **zero crossovers in either direction**:

| Layer | Rows | Licences observed | Crossovers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Checkers, validators and format specs** — what tells you the content is broken | `10` | MIT ×`4`, Apache-2.0 ×`3`, BSD-3-Clause ×`3` | **`0`** |
| **Assistive-technology runtime** — what the disabled student actually runs | `8` | LGPL-2.1, LGPL-3.0, GPL-2.0+, GPL-3.0 ×`3`, MPL-2.0 ×`2` | **`0`** |

🟢 **The rule, stated once so this shelf stops rediscovering it:** *Globant can own and ship the
checking, conversion and reporting layer outright. It cannot vendor the assistive-technology
runtime — it must call it, package it, or deploy it unmodified.* Nothing in this tier forces a
copyleft obligation onto client code **unless somebody links the braille or speech engine into the
product**, and `P248` is the pattern that keeps that from happening by accident.

### 🔴 The copyleft runtime layer — a boundary, not a refusal

These are shelved deliberately. An institution's accessibility posture **is** these programs; a
proposal that omits them is not credible. The licence is a constraint on *linking*, not on *use*.

| Repository | Licence — **byte-read** | Stars | Why it is unavoidable |

### 🔴 `T421` — default branch is load-bearing, and in this tier it is the majority finding

`6` of this tier's repositories do **not** publish from `main` or `master`:

returned `404` on all six and this pass would have reported six fake grant-absences. Read
`default_branch` from the search payload first, always.


## 🆕 `p748` (2026-11-10) — 🔴 **THE ELIGIBILITY-ENGINE LAYER: `9 / 9` AGPL-3.0 ACROSS FOUR REGIONS AND TWO PUBLISHERS, WITH AN MIT AGENT LAYER DIRECTLY ABOVE IT. 🔴 THE `317★` FLAGSHIP CARRIES NO GRANT FILE. 🔴 TWO MIT PAYLOADS HAVE NO TITLE BLOCK.**

**`10` rows · `31` payloads byte-read this pass, `29 / 31` classified from the title block
(`T412`) · `3` grant-absences recorded as `T302` honest negatives with no denominator ·
`28 / 28` addresses existence-confirmed on the raw channel.**

This is the layer under the money: the thing that computes **who is entitled to what**. It is the
most sharply licence-split tier this base has filed — see `T410` — and the split is not along the
language estate (as `T407` found for federation) but along the **layer**: the engine is AGPL-3.0,
the tooling over it is MIT, from the same publishers in the same week. 🟢 **The consequence is an
architecture rather than a refusal, and it is written up as `P242`.**

### The permissive core — what Globant can build on and ship

### The AGPL-3.0 engine layer — a boundary, not a refusal

⚠️ **All nine grants below were byte-read this pass. None is a reason to walk away; each is a reason
to draw the service boundary before the first commit (`P242`).** `AGPL-3.0 §13` bites on
**modification combined with network interaction** — so an *unmodified* upstream engine, run as its
own service behind an API, with your rules and your agent in your own repository, is the posture
that keeps a client's code closed.

## 🆕 `p747` (2026-11-10) — 🔴 **THE FEDERATION LAYER `p736` LEFT BEHIND: `13` ROWS, `6` INDEX CORRECTIONS, AND A LICENCE GRADIENT THAT SPLITS BY *LANGUAGE ESTATE* RATHER THAN BY LAYER. 🟢 A `14,806★` APACHE-2.0 IAM PLATFORM THAT CALLS ITSELF *AGENT-FIRST* WAS AT `0` MENTIONS.**

**`13` rows · `37` payloads byte-read across `42` probed addresses · `36 / 37` classified from the
title block · `6` index values materially corrected, `2` in the *refusing* direction · `5` grant
absences recorded as `T302` honest negatives with a probed-path count and **no** denominator
(`T388`).**

🔵 **The register:** `p736` shelved the SSO *server* (`apereo/cas` and its three clients, above).
This pass shelves **what that server joins** — SAML metadata aggregation, protocol proxying,
attribute release and group/entitlement authority. Every address family in it was at `0` mentions.

### 🟢 `T407` — the licence gradient runs along the *language estate*, and the architectural rule falls straight out

| Estate | Rows read | Payload verdict | Size signature |
|---|--:|---|---|
| **Python** (`IdentityPython`: `pysaml2`, `SATOSA`, `djangosaml2`, `pyop`) | `4 / 4` | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** | `10,758` / `10,177` / `11,357` / `10,177 B` |
| **PHP** (`simplesamlphp`: core + `saml2`) | `2 / 2` | ⚠️ **LGPL-2.1** | `24,487` / `24,459 B` |
| **Java** (`apereo`, held from `p736`) | — | 🟢 Apache-2.0 | — |
| **Platform** (`OpenConext`, `Internet2`, `incommon`, `REANNZ`) | `7 / 7` | 🟢 Apache-2.0 | `10,140`–`11,357 B` |

🟢 **The rule, and it is architectural rather than legal:** **build the Globant-owned components in
Python against `SATOSA` / `pysaml2`, and integrate with SimpleSAMLphp across its *module*
interface rather than forking its core.** The PHP estate is weak copyleft at the library level, so a
module links and a fork infects. This is the same shape as `T381`'s assessment gradient, measured on
a different axis — wired as `P239`.

### 🔴 `T400` — cross-publisher byte-identity is the *norm* for Apache-2.0, so a digest match proves nothing about the publisher

`p746`'s `T392` used a repeated digest as a publisher signature. 🔴 **It does not generalise.**
Digest `58d1e17ffe51` (`11,357 B`) was read this pass on **five unrelated publishers** —
`IdentityPython/djangosaml2`, `Internet2/comanage-registry`, `REANNZ/federationregistry2-Tuakiri`,
`CSCfi/shibboleth-idp-oidc-extension` and `babs/mcp-auth-proxy`. Digest `43070e2d4e53` (`11,356 B`)
appears on `casdoor/casdoor` **and** `OpenConext/eduGAIN-Metadata-Validator`; `c5accbbd8546` on
`Unicon/shibboleth-idp-dockerized` **and** `uchicago/shibboleth-oidc`.
🟢 **Corrected statement of the control:** *within* one publisher a repeated digest is evidence of a
canonical payload (`OpenConext` used `0cec06e0e55f` twice, `IdentityPython` used `ef777cb1e5f4`
twice); *across* publishers it is evidence of nothing but the SPDX text.
🔵 **And `T383` is re-confirmed on a fifth family:** **eight** distinct byte sizes — `10,140`,
`10,173`, `10,177`, `10,758`, `11,323`, `11,337`, `11,356`, `11,357 B` — all classify Apache-2.0.

### 🔴 `T398` — `p746`'s `END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS` discriminator has a false-negative mode, found on first use

`p746` declared `T391`: an Apache-2.0 payload can be the licence concatenated twice, discriminated by
`grep -c 'END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS'`. 🔴 **Used as a *presence* test it fails:**
[`OpenConext/Stepup-saml-bundle`](https://github.com/OpenConext/Stepup-saml-bundle) is a complete,
valid Apache-2.0 body at `10,140 B` that **ends at clause 9 with no appendix and no `END OF TERMS`
line at all** — the grep returns `0`. 🟢 **Corrected rule: `EOT ≥ 2` detects doubling; `EOT = 0`
says nothing.** Classification stays with the title block, which is `36 / 37` here.

## 🆕 `p746` (2026-11-10) — 🔴 **A `252`-REPOSITORY, `361★`, `BSD-3-CLAUSE` EDUCATION-DATA PLATFORM WAS ABSENT FROM THIS ENTIRE BASE. `12` ROWS, AND A LICENCE SEAM THAT CONFIRMS `T360` A THIRD TIME FROM A PUBLISHER THAT HAS NEVER BEEN READ HERE**

> **Pass p746 · `26` payloads byte-read 2026-11-10 over the raw channel, `26 / 26` classified from
> the title block.** The register is **the substrate a national education ministry runs its numbers
> on**. 🔴 **The finding is an absence in this base, not in the market:** `grep` across every live
> shelf returns **`0`** for `dhis2`, `0` for every `unicef/giga-*` address and `0` for
> `CEDS-Collaborative`. All three are live, granted, permissive-or-knowable estates.

🟢 **`T387`'s discipline was applied before a single row was drafted, and it saved the pass from a
false headline.** The obvious candidate for "the US education-data estate" is **Ed-Fi**, and this
pass measured it first: **`469` mentions and `25` distinct `Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS` repositories are
already named on live shelves.** Ed-Fi is not a blind spot. **CEDS, which sits beside it in the same
procurement conversation, is at `0`.** The blind spot was one publisher sideways, exactly as `T387`
predicted it would be.

### 🔴 `T389` — the Giga estate confirms the layer seam a third time, independently, and this one is measured on `6` payloads with zero crossovers

`T360`/`P213` says the licence boundary in education OSS follows **the layer**, not the repository or
the publisher. The **UNICEF–ITU Giga** estate (`32` repositories, school connectivity and school
mapping) is the cleanest instance this base has read, and the only one measured on a whole estate in a single pass:

| Layer | Repositories byte-read | Grant | Payload |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Models, analysis, research** | `giga-global-school-mapping`, `giga-mlab-school-connectivity-cop`, `giga-open-cost-model` | 🟢 **Apache-2.0**, `3 / 3` | `11,357 B` · `c71d239df917` (×2) and `22,713 B` · `a7793c0afb53` |
| **Hosted services and platform backends** | `giga-spatial`, `giga-maps-backend`, `giga-meter-backend`, `project-connect-daily-check-app` | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0**, `4 / 4` | `34,523 B` · `8486a10c4393`; `34,522 B` · `4df3c306ddda`; `34,693 B` · `b25b37948bd2` (the last **shared byte-for-byte** by the Giga Meter client and its backend) |

🔵 **`7 / 7`, zero crossovers, one publisher.** The commercial consequence is immediate and it is the
whole value of the rule: **a studio may take Giga's school-mapping models into a client deliverable
under Apache-2.0, and must not host a derivative of `giga-maps` or `giga-meter`**, because AGPL §13
reaches a *hosted* service rather than a shipped binary. `P233` is built on exactly that boundary.

### 🟢 `T391` — an Apache-2.0 payload can be the licence twice over, and the discriminator is one `grep`

[`unicef/giga-open-cost-model`](https://github.com/unicef/giga-open-cost-model) serves an
`Apache-2.0` of **`22,713 B`** — roughly **double** the `11,357 B` steward text, and far outside the
**`1,184 B`** spread that `693-G` and `p739` established across every Apache payload this base had
read. 🔴 **It is not a variant.** The file contains **`2` occurrences of `END OF TERMS AND
CONDITIONS`** (lines `176` and `377`) and **`8` of `Apache License`**: the licence is **concatenated
with itself**. 🟢 **Rule, cheap, and it should join every Apache read beside `P742-C`'s
conflict-marker check: `grep -c 'END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS'` — `> 1` means a duplicated payload,
not an altered one.** A size heuristic alone would have flagged this as a modified licence and sent
a reader hunting for an added clause that does not exist.

- 🔴 **[`dhis2-metadata/EMIS_AGG`](https://github.com/dhis2-metadata/EMIS_AGG) — the actual education metadata package — carries NO GRANT.** `6` paths probed over the raw channel (`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING`, `LICENSE-MIT`), all `404`; the index agrees (`NONE`). ⚠️ **Recorded as a `T302` honest negative and explicitly NOT as a compliant absence, because the denominator channel is closed this session (`T388`).** 🔵 **This matters more than a `0★` repository normally would:** the platform is `BSD-3-Clause` and **the education configuration on top of it is ungranted**, which is a `P742-B`-shaped split — permissive engine, unlicensed domain content. **Ask the implementing ministry for terms before shipping the package itself; the engine is not in question.**
- 🔴 **`org:Siyavula` — an informed negative on South Africa's best-known open-textbook publisher.** `74` repositories, **`8★` ceiling** (`siyavula.latex2image`, MIT), and the bulk of the estate last touched **2011–2013**. **Not a live estate.** Named so the obvious Africa leg is not re-run a third time.
- 🔴 **The named North America authority list on [`../intel/market.md`](../intel/market.md) is now ENUMERATED, and it is a measured nil** (`T394`). `org:18F` = **`994`** repositories, of which **`2`** are education and **both were last pushed in 2017**; `org:GSA` = `840` repositories, **`0`** education; `org:canada-ca` = `68` repositories, **`0`** education, queried in **both official languages**. ⚠️ **And `3` of the `6` names on that list — `usedgov`, `cdssnc`, `DigitalService` — are not real orgs**, returning the does-not-exist validation error. 🟢 **The standing candidate list is spent; `CEDS-Collaborative-Exchange` is what replaced it and it was not on the list.**
- ⚠️ **Africa country legs returned nothing fileable.** `nigeria school management system` (`59` results) is unlicensed or `1–2★` student and landing-page projects; `kenya education platform open source` (`2`) returns a Congolese media catalogue and a community-health directory; `egypt education ai` (`15`) yields only `revolover00/Bakloriya-AI-SKILLS` (`2★`, pushed `2026-11-09`, **no grant**) and a `3★` demo. 🔵 **Stated as a measured nil with its denominators rather than left silent** — Africa's supply, unlike LATAM's at `T361`, did **not** turn out to be bound to a national substrate that this pass could name. **It is thin, and the one live Africa-relevant estate found is published from Oslo and New York, not from the continent.**

## 🆕 `p745` (2026-11-10) — the assessment and marking estate, and a licence gradient that runs the *opposite* way to the scholarly tier

> **Pass p745 · every grant byte-read 2026-11-10 over the `git` tree channel.** `8` rows. 🟢 **The
> reusable fact this tier produces is a gradient, not a list** (`T381`): in the scholarly tier
> (`p743`) the *framework* was permissive and the *instance* was copyleft. Here it inverts — **the
> standards body publishes Apache-2.0 and MIT, and every deployable implementation is copyleft.**
> So the seam a studio builds on is the **specification library**, never the platform, and `P229`
> is that rule priced out.

### The gradient, measured across the whole tier

| Layer | Publisher | Posture | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Specification** | `1EdTech` (the standards body itself) | 🟢 **permissive** | `lti-1-3-php-library` Apache-2.0 (`124★`), `basiclti-util-java` Apache-2.0 (`60★`), `openbadges-validator-core` Apache-2.0 (`61★`), `cert-schema` **MIT** `1,096 B` `06d87bf42a0a` © *2016 Media Lab Learning* (`77★`), `OpenCASE` Apache-2.0, `digital-credentials-public-validator` Apache-2.0 — *all already shelved; re-confirmed, not re-filed* |
| **Vendor SDK** | `Learnosity`, commercial | 🟢 **permissive** | Apache-2.0 SDKs in `7` languages — a closed-source assessment vendor publishing its whole integration surface permissively |
| **Deployable platform** | `oat-sa` (TAO), `SafeExamBrowser` (ETH Zürich) | 🔴 **copyleft** | GPL-2.0 across `18 / 20` TAO repositories; MPL-2.0 across the SEB estate |
| **LMS plugin** | anyone | 🔴 **copyleft, unavoidably** | `UNIwise/moodle-plagiarism_uniwise` is **GPL-3.0** because it is a *Moodle plugin* — the integration point sets the licence, not the vendor's preference |

🔵 **That last row is the one to carry into a client room.** UNIwise's own infrastructure
repositories are Apache-2.0 and MIT. The moment the same company ships *into Moodle*, the artefact
is GPL-3.0. **A studio's licence answer therefore depends on where it integrates, not on who it
integrates with** — and that is a question answerable at architecture time, before any code exists.

The commit record is a personal Gmail address and the single copyright notice in the tree belongs to **Microsoft**, from a devcontainer file) | University research on **automated correction of open-ended exam questions** — the hardest marking problem in the tier and the one every vendor claims to have solved. 🔴 **Filed with its provenance stated as thin on purpose:** `0★`, `0` forks, default branch `dev` (not `main` — trap #1 of the shared probe, paying out again), last pushed `2024-06-03`. ## 🆕 `p744` (2026-11-10) — the open-scholarship data and format-identification estate: `10` rows, `5` index corrections, and `4` refusals that are *enumerated* rather than assumed

> **Pass p744 · every row byte-read 2026-11-10.** 🟢 **`ACTION p743-A` was run as written** — the
> untried `org:` legs it named were probed, `4` of the `8` are invalid owners from this session
> (`fedora-commons`, `dataverse`, `dariah-eu`, `ubiquitypress` → `422`), `redalyc` resolves with
> `0` repositories, and the remaining three paid. The register was then **extended at the same
> altitude** — the publishers of the open-scholarship record itself — and `34` `org:` legs are
> recorded with their totals in `evidence/org-legs.2026-11-10-p744.tsv`.
>
> 🔵 **Why this tier belongs on a *foundations* page and not on `verticals/`.** None of these is a
> system a client runs; each is a **resolver** — DOI → open copy, string → author identity, bytes
> → file format. They are the components the agent rows on
> [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) call, and three of them are what makes a citation claim
> checkable rather than plausible.
>
> 🔴 **Five index corrections on this page alone, and one goes the expensive way:**
> `openpreserve/fido` `NOASSERTION` → **Apache-2.0**; `openpreserve/jhove` `NOASSERTION` →
> **LGPL-2.1-or-later**; `ORCID/orcid-model` `NOASSERTION` → **not MIT, a bespoke MIT-style grant
> with a trademark clause**; and on [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md)
> `ckan/ckan` `NOASSERTION` → **AGPL-3.0-or-later**. See `T371` and `T377`.

### 🔴 Refused this pass — and these are the first *compliant* absences this base has ever recorded

`ACTION p275` has held since `p275` that a negative grant verdict is only a finding if the **tree**
was enumerated, and `T302` / `736-A2` concluded that this is **impossible in this session** because
`api.github.com/repos/*` is `403` and the HTML channel is blocked. 🟢 **`T374` refutes that: a
blobless, no-checkout, depth-1 `git clone` works here**, `git ls-tree -r` enumerates the complete
tree, and `git cat-file blob` fetches one payload byte-exact. All four refusals below therefore
carry their denominator.

| Refused | Index | Tree entries enumerated | Verdict |
|---|---|--:|---|
| [zotero/translators](https://github.com/zotero/translators) | `none` | **816** | **ABSENCE-ENUMERATED** — the Zotero translator corpus (`1,735★`, `933` forks, the highest-forked thing refused here) publishes **no grant at any depth** |
| [clarin-eric/ParlaMint](https://github.com/clarin-eric/ParlaMint) | `none` | **2,366** | **ABSENCE-ENUMERATED** — EMEA parliamentary corpora; a corpus repository with no software grant in tree |
| [DSpace/xoai](https://github.com/DSpace/xoai) | `none` | **324** | **ABSENCE-ENUMERATED** — ⚠️ and its **default branch is `3.x`**, a fourth specimen of the non-`main` default this base has measured on `jhove` (`integration`), `vufind` (`dev`) and `omeka-s` (`develop`) in this one pass |
| [ror-community/ror-updates](https://github.com/ror-community/ror-updates) | `none` | **100,590** | **ABSENCE-ENUMERATED** — the largest tree enumerated on this base. A curation-ticket repository, not software |


## 🆕 `p741` — the `LGPL-2.1` seam into a `GPL-2.0` estate, and the only permissive QTI 3 code found, `2026-11-10`

> 🟢 **`4` rows, all byte-read today, all promoted out of the backlog, and the first one is worth
> the pass.** `ACTION p739-A` sent this pass at `oat-sa` — the TAO estate, i.e. the **QTI** engine
> this base's own `P204` recommends when the client is an assessment authority. The estate is
> `GPL-2.0`, which Pass 222 already recorded organisation-wide. 🟢 **What `p741` adds is the
> boundary: exactly one component is `LGPL-2.1`, and it is the LTI integration bundle** — so a
> proprietary service may *link* it rather than inherit from it, and the seam into a client's TAO
> becomes permissive even though TAO is not (`T356`, `P211`).
>
> 🔵 **The second theme is a deliberately small bet against a copyleft wall.**
> [`kennisnet/php-qti3`](https://github.com/kennisnet/php-qti3) is the **only permissive QTI 3
> implementation this pass found** anywhere. ⚠️ **It has `0★`, `0` forks and is `9` months old, and
> that is stated in its row rather than buried** — it is a watch item and a last-resort build base,
> not a dependency to ship. Filed because `T357`'s enumeration of the whole `kennisnet` owner
> (`25` repositories, max `4★`) found nothing larger, and because naming it lets a successor
> re-measure it instead of re-searching for it.


## 🆕 `p743` (2026-11-10) — the scholarly and research-data infrastructure estate, and `16` rows from a tier this base had never addressed

> 🟢 **This is a tier, not a cohort.** Of the `11` publisher addresses probed before any row was
> written, **`10` appeared on no shelf of this base and `0` appeared in the `3,382`-row
> `evidence/address-ledger.tsv`** (measurement committed at
> `evidence/tier-novelty.2026-11-10-p743.tsv`). The eleventh, `ilios/ilios`, appears **only in the
> trending logs** — discovered and byte-read by an earlier pass and never promoted (`P210` class). The publishers are not obscure: **CERN** (Invenio, the framework
> under Zenodo), **Harvard IQSS** (Dataverse), **Samvera**, **Islandora**, **PKP** (Open Journal
> Systems), **SciELO** and **LA Referencia**. Denominators, all read this pass from
> `gh api -X GET search/repositories`: `org:inveniosoftware` **`166`**, `org:IQSS` **`183`**,
> `org:samvera` **`28`**, `org:Islandora` **`54`**, `org:pkp` **`87`**, `org:scieloorg` **`158`**,
> `org:lareferencia` **`38`**; `topic:research-data-management` **`266`**,
> `topic:institutional-repository` **`89`**.
> 🔵 **The cause is discovery vocabulary, and it generalises `p736`'s finding that phrase-led
> discovery finds applications and never infrastructure (`T363`).** This tier does not describe
> itself with the word *education*. It says *research data*, *institutional repository*, *scholarly
> publishing*. Every leg this base has ever run asked for the first word.
> 🟢 **Licences are better than the LMS layer, not worse:** of the `16` rows below, `11` are MIT,
> `3` BSD-2-Clause, `4` Apache-2.0 (one byte-canonical) and `1` GPL-3.0 by notice.
> 🔴 **`3` index values corrected from the bytes** — `IQSS/dataverse` and `samvera/hyku` both index
> `NOASSERTION` and are **Apache-2.0**; `lareferencia/dspace-stats-collector` indexes `NOASSERTION`
> and is **GPL-3.0 by notice** (`T367`, `T368`).

## 🆕 `p740` — the xAPI specification estate and the Ed-Fi pipeline, `14` rows promoted out of the backlog, `2026-11-10`

> 🟢 **This pass promoted the substrate that this base's own market intelligence has been calling
> the prerequisite for AI in education, and it was sitting unread in the backlog.** The 2026
> reading across 1EdTech, A4L and the OECD is consistent: AI in education is bottlenecked on
> **AI-ready learning data** — consistent formats, consistent definitions, timely availability —
> and the two standards that carry it are **xAPI with a Learning Record Store** for learner
> activity and **Ed-Fi** for K-12 system-of-record data. Both have a complete permissive reference
> implementation. Neither was on this shelf.
>
> 🟢 **`adlnet` is the publisher of the xAPI specification itself** — the ADL Initiative, a US
> Department of Defense programme — and its estate is `Apache-2.0` with an `MIT` conformance test
> suite. **`edanalytics` is the Ed-Fi tooling estate**, `Apache-2.0` for the pipeline and
> `MPL-2.0` for the dbt packages. 🔴 **Four licence surprises, all byte-read, all consequential:**
> an `Apache-2.0` payload whose operative text does not match the others (`T349`), four `MPL-2.0`
> payloads that a naive classifier reads as `GPL` (`T345`), a US federal agency releasing a
> reference implementation into the **public domain** under the Unlicense, and an `MIT` grant
> living at `MIT-LICENSE` after four conventional paths returned `404` (`T347`).
