## 🆕 `p739` — `6` rows promoted out of the backlog, `2026-11-10`

> 🟢 **The headline is a substrate, not a model: the `Apache-2.0` data standard that every US K-12
> integration in the country already speaks.** `p739`'s promotion census surfaced
> **`19` repositories under `Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS`, `30` in the organisation, and every one
> `Apache-2.0`** — and this base had shelved the *implementations* (`Ed-Fi-ODS`,
> `Data-Management-Service`, `edfi-oneroster`) while leaving the **standard itself** in the
> backlog for `19` passes. That is `P693-A`'s promotion failure in its most expensive form: the
> piece a client's architects actually read was the piece nobody filed.
>
> 🔵 **A gate finding that holds in both directions, measured on `3` fresh Apache-2.0 payloads.**
> `Ed-Fi-Data-Standard` `10,173 B`, `Utdanningsdirektoratet/PAS2-Public` `11,325 B`,
> `yetanalytics/datasim` `11,357 B` — **a spread of `1,184 B`** — all three normalise to
> **`8,967` chars of operative §1–§9 text at the identical digest `sha256₁₂ b7e7e94fb666`**. This
> reproduces `693-G` a second time independently, and **extends it to a payload that drops the
> `APPENDIX` entirely** (`Ed-Fi`: `appendix=NO`), which p693's four payloads could not test.
> **A `−1,184 B` residual and a `0 B` residual carry the same information about the terms: none.**
> Evidence: `evidence/apache-operative-normalisation.2026-11-10-p739.tsv`.

| Repo | Stars | Licence (gate verdict · file · raw bytes) | Last push | Region | What it is, and what it is for |
|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| [Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-Data-Standard](https://github.com/Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/Ed-Fi-Data-Standard) | 46 | **Apache-2.0** · `LICENSE` · `10,173 B` · sha256₁₂ `a6cba85bc92e` · operative `8,967` chars | **2026-10-27** | **North America** | 🟢 **The data model US K-12 interoperability is actually built on, and the single most useful thing on this page for a North American engagement.** Not a library — a **specification** (`Mermaid`-rendered domain models, `created 2020-04-24`, `14` forks) describing students, staff, sections, enrolments, assessments, attendance and discipline as one normalised schema, which the state and district systems in the Ed-Fi estate then implement. 🔵 **Why it belongs on the foundations shelf rather than the platform shelf:** you do not deploy it, you *conform to it* — and conforming is what makes an AI layer installable in a district without a bespoke integration per source system. 🟢 **The estate around it is live and all `Apache-2.0`:** `Ed-Fi-ODS` (`28★`, pushed **2026-11-10**) is the operational data store and API, `Data-Management-Service` (`12★`, pushed **2026-11-10**) is the named successor API platform, `edfi-oneroster` (`6★`) serves a **OneRoster 1.2** API straight off an ODS database — so a studio can meet a district on whichever standard its vendors already speak. 🔵 **This is the answer to `p738`'s `6%`-clear-policy finding at the technical layer:** the reason a North American AI pilot does not reach production is usually roster and assessment plumbing, and this is the plumbing, pre-agreed. ⚠️ A standard moves slowly and by committee; read the version matrix before promising conformance. ⚠️ Apache-2.0 is permissive but carries **§4 attribution and NOTICE duties** — satisfiable, not ignorable. |
| [yetanalytics/datasim](https://github.com/yetanalytics/datasim) | 23 | **Apache-2.0** · `LICENSE` · `11,357 B` · sha256₁₂ `c71d239df917` · operative `8,967` chars | **2026-10-16** | **North America** | 🟢 **An xAPI statement generator: it simulates a cohort of learners producing realistic learning-record traffic, driven by xAPI Profiles.** Clojure, `created 2019-08-15`, `13` forks. 🟢 **Filed because it solves the problem that kills learning-analytics pilots before they start — there is no data yet.** A dashboard, a dropout model or an at-risk alert cannot be built, demoed or load-tested against an empty Learning Record Store, and real learner records are the hardest data in this industry to get cleared. `datasim` produces **profile-conformant synthetic traffic with no personal data in it at all**, which also means it needs no DPIA, no FERPA review and no ethics board to run. 🔵 **The composition is first-party and complete:** generate with `datasim` → store in [`yetanalytics/lrsql`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql) (`144★`, Apache-2.0, pushed 2026-11-08, already shelved) → forward with `xapipe` (Apache-2.0, shelved). See `P201`. ⚠️ Clojure/JVM; the realism is only as good as the Profile you feed it. ⚠️ Synthetic data validates **pipelines**, never model accuracy — do not report a metric earned on it. |
| [digitalcredentials/signing-service](https://github.com/digitalcredentials/signing-service) | 5 | **MIT** · `LICENSE` · `1,087 B` · sha256₁₂ `ca28a7ae5c7e` · holder `Digital Credentials Consortium` | 2025-07-31 | **North America** | 🟢 **A minimal VC-API signing service for Verifiable Credentials, published by the Digital Credentials Consortium — the MIT-hosted university consortium, not a vendor.** Node.js, `created 2023-02-16`, `8` forks. 🔵 **Filed as the smallest usable piece of the credential stack:** it does one thing — sign and revoke — so it drops into an existing registrar system instead of replacing it, which is the only way credential work gets approved at a university. 🟢 **Posture is unusually clean for this shelf: `92` repositories in the organisation, MIT throughout, `1,087 B` payloads shared verbatim across them** (`verifier-plus` and `open-badges-context` are byte-identical at sha256₁₂ `85569d7ed8fc`) — one licence review covers the estate. 🔵 **Why it matters commercially:** `1EdTech`'s own 2026 reading is that institutions are moving to *verified digital credentials aligned with workforce needs*, and this is that, in code, from a consortium a provost already recognises. ⚠️ Last pushed `2025-07-31` — the quietest row in this group; read before depending. |
| [digitalcredentials/open-badges-context](https://github.com/digitalcredentials/open-badges-context) | 6 | **MIT** · `LICENSE` · `1,087 B` · sha256₁₂ `85569d7ed8fc` · holder `Digital Credentials Consortium` | 2025-05-21 | **North America** | 🟢 **The OBv3 (Open Badges v3) JSON-LD context, as an npm package.** `created 2022-04-07`. 🔵 **The least glamorous row on this page and the one most likely to be needed:** every Open Badges v3 credential must resolve its `@context`, and resolving it over the network at verification time is both a latency and an availability dependency. Vendoring this package removes it. 🟢 **Filed to make the credential recipe in `P201` self-contained** — `signing-service` + this context + `verifier-plus` is a complete issue-and-verify loop with no proprietary component. ⚠️ A context file tracks a spec; pin the version and re-check it when OBv3 revises. ⚠️ `2` forks, pushed `2025-05-21`. |
| [project-sunbird/sunbird-quml-player](https://github.com/project-sunbird/sunbird-quml-player) | 6 | **MIT** · `LICENSE` · `1,072 B` · sha256₁₂ `47022c44beee` · holder `Project Sunbird` | 2023-02-08 | **APAC** | 🟢 **A JavaScript player for QuML question sets — the assessment-item renderer from the stack behind India's national school platform.** `created 2020-10-21`. 🔴 **Read the forks, not the stars, and this is a new measurement for this base: `6★` against `39` forks — a `6.5 : 1` fork-to-star ratio.** Stars measure admiration; forks of a renderer measure **deployment**, because an organisation adapting an item player forks it. `sunbird-collection-editor` is sharper still at `3★ / 45` forks (`15 : 1`). 🟢 **So this is the APAC counterpart to `Khan/perseus` (`T329`)**: a battle-tested, MIT, deterministic item renderer — but one aligned to **QuML**, the question markup the Sunbird/DIKSHA estate and its state deployments already author in. For an Indian engagement that is not a preference, it is the format the content exists in. 🔵 **Sunbird is MIT across all `77` repositories read this pass** — a uniform grant over a national-scale stack is rare and makes licence review a single exercise. ⚠️ Pushed `2023-02-08`: stable rather than active, which for a renderer is defensible and for a security dependency is not. ⚠️ QuML is not QTI; see `P202` for which to pick. |
| [bigdata-ustc/CAT4AI](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/CAT4AI) | 8 | **MIT** · `LICENSE` · `1,062 B` · sha256₁₂ `f54657b48631` · holder `Copyright (c) 2019 tswsxk` | 2024-10-01 | **APAC** | 🟢 **Computerised Adaptive Testing applied to evaluating AI models rather than people — psychometrics as an LLM-evaluation method, from the USTC lab whose `EduKTM` and `EduCDM` this base already holds.** Jupyter, `created 2024-08-06`. 🔵 **Filed because it inverts this shelf's usual direction and that inversion is sellable.** Every other adaptive-testing row here measures a *learner*; this one uses the same IRT machinery to measure a *model* — selecting the next benchmark item by current ability estimate instead of running the whole suite. For an education client that must justify *which* model it put in front of students, that is a defensible, published method rather than a vendor leaderboard. 🟢 **Composes directly with the lab's held rows:** `EduCAT` (MIT, `77★`) for the learner-facing selection, `EduCDM` (Apache-2.0, `203★`) for the IRT/MIRT implementations. 🔵 **Note the holder: `tswsxk`, an individual, not the laboratory** — the `Ansarimajid/College-ERP` class (`p683`). The MIT grant stands; anything beyond it is a conversation with a person, not with USTC. ⚠️ `8★`, `2` forks, pushed `2024-10-01` — research code, and `738-A` measured this lab's education-subject repositories as **`4 / 4` ungranted**, so its permissive rows are the exception rather than the rule. |


## Khan Academy's own assessment engine, the NSF data-science estate, and the Indic lecture stack 🆕 new section at `p738`



**`13` rows, every licence read from the bytes today, every one permissive or public-domain.**
🟢 **The finding that reorganises this page: the single most-used piece of open-source assessment
software in the world was never on it.** [`Khan/perseus`](https://github.com/Khan/perseus) is
**MIT**, `1,598★`, and was pushed **today** — it is the exercise-and-question engine that renders
and grades every interactive item on Khan Academy, and this base has spent dozens of passes
declaring an assessment-component gap while it sat in the open under the most permissive grant
there is. Three of this pass's publishers (`Khan`, `concord-consortium`, `AI4Bharat`) are
**first-party operators of systems with tens of millions of learners**, publishing the components
of those systems rather than demos of them — which is the distinction `T326` was reaching for.

React/TypeScript, published as versioned npm packages. 🟢 **This is the component every grading and practice pattern on this base has been assuming and none has named.** An LLM can write an item and mark prose; it cannot render a draggable number line, validate an algebraic expression as *equivalent* rather than *identical*, or score a graph the learner dragged. Perseus does all three, has done at Khan Academy's scale for a decade, and is MIT. 🔵 **Pair it with an LLM as the item *author* and Perseus as the item *runtime*** — the model generates the Perseus JSON, the engine renders and scores it deterministically, and the score is defensible to a registrar because no model is in the marking path (`P200`). ⚠️ A front-end library, not an application: no item bank, no gradebook, no auth. ⚠️ Khan's own stack is the reference consumer, so some widgets assume Khan conventions; budget integration time. |

### 🟢 `738-D` — the licence gate called CC0 a CC-BY licence, `3 / 3`, and that error ran in the expensive direction

🔴 **`p736`'s gate classified every **CC0-1.0** payload as `CC-BY-family (not a software grant)` —
measured `3 / 3`**: [`GEANT/edugain-openidfed`](https://github.com/GEANT/edugain-openidfed)
(`7,048 B`), [`dini-ag-kim/amb`](https://github.com/dini-ag-kim/amb) (`7,048 B`) and
[`openjournals/jose-reviews`](https://github.com/openjournals/jose-reviews) (`6,555 B`). The cause
is the rule order: the generic `Creative Commons` pattern fires before anything can recognise CC0.

🔴 **This is not a cosmetic mislabel. CC0 is a public-domain dedication — it imposes *no*
obligation, not even attribution — so it is strictly *more* permissive than CC-BY and more
permissive than MIT.** The old verdict's parenthetical, *"not a software grant"*, then pushes the
most freely usable material this base encounters into the cannot-use bucket. Two of the three
payloads are exactly the kind of asset a studio wants: a national metadata profile and a
federation specification.

🟢 **Repaired and published as `P197`**, with the controls in
`evidence/gate-controls.2026-11-10-p738.tsv`: `CC0 1.0 Universal` is matched **above** the generic
rule, and the bare preamble line `Creative Commons Legal Code` is treated as decoration so it
cannot terminate the walk — **both layouts occur** (two payloads carry the preamble, one begins
`CC0 1.0 Universal` directly), so the fix must survive both and does. **`27 / 27` payloads
classified this pass, `3` corrected, `24` unchanged, `0` regressions** — including the `T311`
GPL-§13 control, which still returns `GPL-3.0` and not AGPL.

### 🔴 `738-E` — "no grant found" was wrong twice, and the filename chain is why

🔴 **Two repositories that do carry a grant were scored *grant-not-found* by `p736`'s six-name
chain, and both were recovered by extending it:**

| Repo | Found at | Raw bytes | Verdict | Why the old chain missed it |
|---|---|--:|---|---|
| [iansan5653/open-mcr](https://github.com/iansan5653/open-mcr) | **`license.txt`** | 32,423 | GPL-3.0 | lowercase; the raw probe is case-sensitive (`724-I`) |
| [kiwix/kiwix-js](https://github.com/kiwix/kiwix-js) | **`LICENSE-GPLv3.txt`** | 35,147 | GPL-3.0 | suffixed filename, not in the chain |

🟢 **The chain is extended from `6` names to `14` at `P197`, and the lowercase variants are
load-bearing rather than defensive** — one of the two recoveries is a lowercase filename.
🔵 **And the honest-negative discipline earned its keep**: because `T302` requires the verdict to
be written *"no grant found at the probed paths"* rather than *"no grant"*, these two were
recoverable rather than wrongly refused. ⚠️ `open-mcr` is **GPL-3.0** optical-mark recognition for
paper multiple-choice sheets — genuinely useful in low-connectivity assessment, last pushed 2023 —
and it is recorded here as a **read-only reference**, not filed as a build-on component.

### 🔴 `738-F` — a manifest licence can be npm's default value, which turns `T312` into a two-step rule

🔴 **[`cast-org/clusive`](https://github.com/cast-org/clusive)** — CAST's UDL adaptive reading
environment, `16★`, pushed 2026-04-22, indexed `NOASSERTION` — **has no licence file at any of
`14` probed paths**, and its `package.json` declares `"license": "ISC"`. 🔴 **`ISC` is the value
`npm init` writes when nobody chooses one.** Its `README.md` §License says something different and
specific: *"made available under the BSD 3-clause license"*.

🟢 **`T312` said: when there is no licence file, read the manifest. `p738` adds the second half:
do not trust the manifest alone.** The repaired order is **licence file → README licence section →
manifest**, and a manifest-only value must be corroborated before it is recorded — `ISC` and
`MIT` in a `package.json` with no licence file are the two scaffold defaults and carry the least
evidential weight of anything the gate reads. 🔵 Clusive is recorded as **BSD-3-Clause on the
README's own statement**, flagged as uncorroborated by a payload, and is filed on
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) with that caveat attached rather than here.
## European research-and-education network estates, and the permissive execution sandbox 🆕 new section at `p737`

**`10` rows, all read `2026-11-10`, all live, `0` archived, `10 / 10` licence verdicts decided from the
payload.** 🟢 **This section exists because `p737` attacked `T290` — "`0` EMEA-authored learning
agents", declared two passes running — and found that the EMEA supply is real, substantial and
permissive, but that it is *not shaped like an agent*. It is shaped like infrastructure, and it is
published by national research-and-education network operators and university IT centres rather
than by startups.** **all `10`** rows below come from `org:` legs against publishers this base had
never addressed: `openHPI` (Hasso Plattner Institute, Germany), `CSCfi` (Finland's national IT
centre for science), `1024pix` and `betagouv` (French state), `serlo` (German non-profit),
`virtUOS` (Universität Osnabrück) and `ELEVATE-Project` (Indian DPI). 🔵 **`T314`'s rule — `org:` legs produce
rows and prose legs do not — held again at `p737` and is now `53` legs deep: all `14` fileable rows
of this pass came from `org:` legs, and the `9` mandated prose legs produced `0`** (`T326`).

> 🟢 **The two rows to read first are `codeocean` and `poseidon`, and the reason is that together
> they close a hole every AI-grading pattern on this base has been quietly standing over.** An LLM
> that marks code must *run* the code, and running a stranger's submission needs a sandbox with a
> queue, resource limits and a scheduler. This base held graders, rubrics and MCP servers; it held
> **no permissive execution substrate**. `codeocean` is **BSD-3-Clause** and `poseidon` is **MIT** —
> both from a named German university institute, both in production for that institute's own MOOC
> platform. See `P192`.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Region | What it is, and why it is a building block |
|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| [openHPI/codeocean](https://github.com/openHPI/codeocean) | 73 | **BSD-3-Clause** | 2026-09-28 | **EMEA** | **An automated assessment platform for web-based programming exercises** — learners write code in the browser, it is executed against the teacher's test suite, and scores plus per-test feedback come back. Built and run by the **Hasso Plattner Institute** for openHPI, its own MOOC platform, so it is load-bearing software for a real cohort rather than a demo. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,550 B` raw, `Redistribution and use in source and binary forms` **with** a `Neither the name` clause → **BSD-3-Clause**; index agrees. 🟢 **Filed as the most reusable EMEA row of this pass.** Every AI-marking recipe this base publishes assumes something safely executes the submission; this is that something, permissively licensed, from an institutional publisher, with the pedagogy already modelled (exercise, test suite, feedback, score). 🔵 **It is also the honest answer to a client who asks why an LLM alone cannot grade a programming assignment:** the model can read the code, but only an execution harness can tell you it compiles and passes. Pair the two — `P192`. ⚠️ Ruby on Rails, and opinionated: it is an application with its own data model, not a library you link. ⚠️ **BSD, so not certified by digest** (`T288`); bytes recorded and the operative clause named. 🟢 Region placed on a named German institutional publisher. |

Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,157 B` raw, `Permission is hereby granted, free of charge` → **MIT**; index agrees. 🟢 **An `11★` row filed above far more popular repositories, for the same reason `p736` filed a `2★` university release: it is the only MIT-licensed, purpose-built, production-proven code-execution sandbox for assessment that this base has found.** The generic alternatives are a container runtime and a weekend of your own scheduling code. 🔵 **Separable from `codeocean` by design** — the MIT grant and the HTTP API mean you can drive it from your own grader without adopting the Rails application, which is exactly what makes `P192` a three-week pattern rather than a fork. ⚠️ Last pushed March 2026 and `11★`: read it as a stable component with a small maintainer base, and pin your version. ⚠️ Needs Nomad, which is real operational surface. 🟢 Region as above. |

This is a maintained, nationally-deployed implementation of exactly that workflow. 🔵 **Generalises past research data without modification:** the same approve-record-expire loop governs access to a student-records corpus used for training, which is the first question a university's data-protection officer asks. 🔵 Clojure, actively pushed, deployed by a national operator — unusually solid provenance for a governance component. ⚠️ Not education-specific and not AI-specific; it is the workflow, and you supply the education semantics. 🟢 Region placed on a named Finnish national institution. |

Gate: `LICENSE`, `35,149 B` raw, family line `GNU GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE` with the version line resolving to **`3.0`** → **GPL-3.0**; index agrees. 🔵 **This row is `p737`'s live `T311` control and the reason the gate's version resolution was built: a whole-payload family match scores this payload `AGPL` because GPL-3.0 §13 is titled "Use with the GNU Affero General Public License", and the `p736` gate scored it a bare `GPL` with no version at all. It is GPL-3.0** — which matters, because the AGPL's network clause would reach a hosted deployment and the GPL's does not. 🟢 **Filed on its own merit too, for a shape this base had not recorded: a European university running its own LLM chat service in production and publishing the operational tooling for it.** The interesting artefact is not the exporter, it is the evidence that institution-hosted LLM services have reached the stage of needing dashboards and alerting — which is the question a client asks immediately after "can we self-host". 🔵 It is the observability component of `P195`. 🔴 **Posture: GPL-3.0, so deploy it as-is and do not fork it into a product**; distributing a modified version triggers the obligation. ⚠️ `40★`, narrow by design, and LibreChat-specific. 🟢 Region placed on a named German university publisher. |

### 🟢 `737-A` — `T290` was the wrong question, and the right one has an answer

For two passes this base has recorded **"`0` EMEA-authored learning agents"** as a supply gap.
`p737` tested it and the finding is a **reframing, not a correction**: the count is right and the
inference drawn from it was wrong. **EMEA's open education supply is not thin — it is
infrastructural.** `10` rows above, `9` of them permissive, every one from a national research network,
a university IT centre, a state digital service or a non-profit platform, and **not one of them is
an agent**.

🔵 **Why this is a commercially useful distinction rather than a taxonomic one.** An engagement
that needs a tutor can take one from the saturated APAC/NA agent shelf under MIT and localise it.
An engagement that needs *the thing a European institution is allowed to deploy* — a sandbox that
executes student code, an access-approval record, a defensible model-selection method, an openly
licensed German content API — could not be served from this base at all before this pass. **EMEA
publishes the half of the stack that regulation makes mandatory and that nobody writes listicles
about**, which is also why `9` prose legs found none of it (`T326`).

### 🔴 `737-B` — the licence gate gained a version, and one held verdict was incomplete

`p736` made the clause-based gate mandatory and it is holding: **`20` payloads read at `p737`,
`20 / 20` classified, `20 / 20` agreeing with the search index** where the index has a value, and
the `T311` trap passed again (`1024pix/pix` is AGPL-3.0 **by its family line**, and
`virtUOS/librechat_exporter` is **GPL-3.0 and not AGPL** despite GPL-3.0 §13 naming the AGPL).

🔴 **But the `p736` gate returned a bare `GPL` / `LGPL`, discarding the version, and that is not a
usable verdict.** GPL-2.0 and GPL-3.0 differ on patents and on compatibility, so "GPL" does not tell
a studio whether a component can be combined with what it already ships. `p737` resolves the version
from the version line that **follows** the family line — never from the file size, which `T313`
already disqualified and which would fail here specifically: **GPL-3.0 payloads measured `35,149 B`
and `35,147 B` against AGPL-3.0's `34,280 B`, `2.5 %` apart.** Measured on `4 / 4` GPL-family
payloads, of which **`oat-sa/qti-sdk` is GPL-2.0** — a materially different verdict the previous
gate could not express. Runnable at `evidence/licence-gate.p737.py`; `18`-row truth table with both
gates' output side by side at `evidence/licence-gate-controls.2026-11-10-p737.tsv`.

🔵 **`T312` re-confirmed `1 / 1` on a fresh probe:** `kstateome/canvas-api` still returns `404` on
all six licence filenames while the index correctly reports LGPL-3.0 from `pom.xml`. A missing
licence file is still not evidence of a missing grant.

### 🟢 `736-A` — the identity layer was never missing from the market, only from this base

Stated plainly because it is a **process** finding and the next pass should act on it. `apereo/cas`
is **`14` years old, `11,393★`, Apache-2.0 and the de facto SSO server of higher education**. It is
not obscure, not new and not hard to find: the leg that returned it was **`org:apereo`**, a single
query against a foundation whose other projects (Sakai, Opencast) this base already holds.

🔴 **The `3,343`-address ledger did not contain it, nor `phpCAS`, nor `java-cas-client`, nor
`cas-initializr` — and `org:apereo` returned `30` repositories of which `28` were novel.** Three
patterns had already identified identity as the integration point. 🔵 **The generalisable lesson:
this base's discovery has been phrase-led (`tutoring agent`, `grading rubric LLM`) and
phrase-led discovery finds *applications*, never *infrastructure*, because infrastructure is not
described with the vertical's vocabulary.** `apereo/cas`'s description does not contain the word
*education*. 🟢 **Remedy, and it is cheap: enumerate the known institutional publishers by
`org:`/`user:` rather than describing what you want.** The `org:` legs of this pass (`apereo`,
`h5p`, `instructure`, `oppia`, `sakaiproject`, `1EdTech`, `moodlehq`, `ILIAS-eLearning`,
`ucfopen`) produced **every one of this section's `14` rows**; the four mandated prose legs
produced **`0`** — the `40`th consecutive nil.

### 🔴 `736-B` — ILIAS's unlicensed-scaffold finding (`T292`) now has its denominator

`p734` recorded that ILIAS demonstrates its own extension points with unlicensed scaffolds.
`org:ILIAS-eLearning` returns **`16` repositories** and the measurement is now exact:

| Class | Count | Licence |
|---|--:|---|
| `ILIAS` itself | 1 | GPL-3.0 (`507★`, pushed `2026-11-09`) |
| `Test*` / `*Example` plugin-slot scaffolds | 9 | **`5` carry no licence, `4` carry GPL-3.0** |
| Style, image, docs and site repositories | 6 | **`4` carry no licence**, 2 GPL-3.0 |

🔴 **`9 / 16` of the estate carries no grant at all, and `5` of those `9` are precisely the
scaffolds a developer must copy to build an ILIAS plugin** (`TestRepositoryObject`,
`FieldTypeHookExample`, `TestCalendarCustomGrid`, `TestCalendarCustomModal`, `TestPDFRenderer`).
🔵 **Consequence for an EMEA engagement, stated as guidance:** ILIAS is a significant
German-speaking-market LMS, but **you cannot lawfully start an ILIAS plugin from its own published
example** — the example has no grant, and the platform it extends is GPL-3.0. The LTI route
(`P186`) and the H5P content-type route (`P190`) both avoid this entirely, and for ILIAS
specifically they are not merely cleaner but the only defensible options.

## LTI interop, knowledge-tracing models and courseware harnesses 🆕 new section at `p735`

**`5` rows, all read `2026-11-10`, all live.** Substrates rather than deployable systems. 🟢 **The
first row is the most reusable thing this pass found anywhere**, and it is not an AI component at
all — it is the **integration surface** every AI component eventually needs. 🔴 **Two of the five
carry BSD grants and are therefore filed with bytes recorded and no stock claim made**, per `T288`:
the gate certifies MIT and Apache only, and `p735` leaves that restriction exactly where `p734` put
it (see `P184`).

Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,320 B` raw → `1283 B`/`fdb05aeb1085`. ⚠️ **Not certified — BSD, per `T288`**; the payload was read and the bytes are recorded, and no stock claim is made from them. 🟢 **Why this is the row to take from `p735`: LTI is the only door into an institution's LMS, and this base had no readable implementation of it.** Every pattern on [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) that ends "…and surface it in the LMS" has been hand-waving that last step. Moodle is GPL-3.0, Open edX and Frappe LMS are AGPL-3.0, Canvas is AGPL-3.0 — so the *integration* has always been where the licence problem bites, and **LTI is how you integrate without deriving from any of them**. A permissively-licensed, production-grade LTI app is therefore the cleanest answer this base holds to "how does this reach students". 🔵 **Second reason, and it is a regulatory one:** the repository ships `docs/setting-up-a-region.md` — **geographic region setup as a first-class documented operation**, which is the data-residency requirement EMEA procurement asks about and almost no ed-tech repository answers in its docs. 🟢 Pushed the day before it was read. |

Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,066 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 **Filed because knowledge tracing is the learner model, and a learner model is the one component a studio cannot fake in a demo.** This base holds BKT (`OATutor`) and FSRS (`rs-fsrs`); this adds a **peer-reviewed deep-KT baseline with a permissive grant** — and `p689`'s gap `(2)` recorded that the pedagogical-evaluation repositories it probed ship **no grant at all**, `2 / 2`. This one does. 🔵 **An honest detail that raises rather than lowers confidence:** the README credits two outside contributors for identifying and fixing a **data-leak defect** in the original evaluation code. A KT benchmark that has publicly repaired a leak is worth more than one that has never been audited — but any figure reproduced from it must come from the post-fix code. ⚠️ Research code: no serving layer, no API. ⚠️ Region unplaced — English README, international venue, US-origin datasets; author nationality is not a region placement. |
| [DavidMiserak/GoCard](https://github.com/DavidMiserak/GoCard) | 59 | MIT | 2026-05-15 | Global ⚠️ | A **file-based spaced-repetition system in Go**: flashcards are plain **Markdown files in ordinary directories**, so the entire review corpus is diffable, reviewable and version-controlled in Git, driven from a keyboard-first terminal interface. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,070 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 **This is the exact complement to `p734`'s `rs-fsrs` row, and together they close `T265`.** `p731` established that spaced repetition has commoditised and the durable asset is the **capture surface**, not the scheduler; `p734` then filed the scheduler as a reusable Rust core. **`GoCard` is the capture surface** — and because its store is Markdown-on-disk rather than a proprietary deck format, a client's existing content, an LLM's generated cards and a human editor's corrections all write to the same place. 🔵 Pairs directly with `WordNest` on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md): collect by hand, schedule with a component, review in Git. ⚠️ Single-maintainer; terminal-first, so no learner-facing web or mobile surface. ⚠️ Region unplaced. |
| [infolab-kaist/KiteProject](https://github.com/infolab-kaist/KiteProject) | 18 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-04-20 | **APAC** | **KAIST Infolab's task-driven educational project** — a three-stage hands-on data-systems curriculum (**KiteSQL** for query and schema work, **KiteRAG** for retrieval-augmented generation over vector search, **KiteDB** for database internals as a capstone), delivered through **GitHub Classroom with automated grading and competitive leaderboards**. Gate: `LICENSE`, `11,337 B` raw → **`9942 B`/`3fda30957805`** — 🔵 **stock Apache at `19 B` BELOW `p734`'s published stock raw band floor of `11,356 B`** (`T300`), which is the clearest single demonstration this base has that **raw size is not a proxy for stock-ness and only the gate decides**. 🟢 **Filed as a courseware *harness*, which is a category this page did not have:** the reusable asset is not the SQL content, it is the **assignment-distribution plus autograding plus leaderboard loop**, published Apache-2.0 by a named national research university. That is the delivery mechanism for every "upskill the client's engineers" engagement, and it already exists. 🔵 Composes with `belajar-ngoding-byGanzu` (tests as examiner) and `codebreaker` (competitive grading) into one gradeable-cohort story. ⚠️ Curriculum is database- and RAG-specific; the harness generalises, the three projects do not. 🟢 Region placed on a named Korean institutional publisher. |
| [adaptive-machine-learning/CapyMOA](https://github.com/adaptive-machine-learning/CapyMOA) | 153 | BSD-3-Clause | **2026-11-08** | Global ⚠️ | **Stream machine learning in Python** — classification, regression, clustering, anomaly detection and semi-supervised learning over *data streams*, with evaluators built for the streaming setting, descending from the long-established MOA toolkit. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,509 B` raw → `1459 B`/`4f44b17a7ff4`. ⚠️ **Not certified — BSD, per `T288`.** 🔵 **Filed for a specific and narrow reason: learning analytics is a streaming problem and this base has been treating it as a batch one.** Engagement data arrives continuously and the model must update without a nightly retrain — which is what prequential evaluation and concept-drift detection are for, and both are first-class here. A dropout-risk or engagement model built on batch scikit-learn pipelines silently assumes a stationary cohort, and a cohort is never stationary across a term. ⚠️ **Not an education library** — no education row, dataset or example ships in it; the fit is methodological and a studio must supply the education framing. ⚠️ Region unplaced. |

## Retention schedulers, platform scaffolds and regional compliance components 🆕 new section at `p734`

**`5` rows, all read `2026-11-10`.** Substrates rather than deployable systems. **`4` are live;
`1` is archived and is filed with that status in its first sentence**, because an archived
repository is still a valid grant and its code is still reusable — what it is not is maintained.
🟢 **Licence verification this pass is stronger than the row count suggests:** the Apache constant
`p733` declared unreproducible is computed here (`9942 B` / `3fda30957805`) and **two of the five
payloads below are part of the `5`-publisher set that establishes it** (`T287`).

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Region | What it is, and why it is a building block |
|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| [open-spaced-repetition/rs-fsrs](https://github.com/open-spaced-repetition/rs-fsrs) | 53 | MIT | 2026-07-20 | Global | The **FSRS spaced-repetition scheduler implemented in Rust** — the scheduling core on its own, with no UI, no store and no learner model attached. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,079 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🔵 **This is the row `T265` asked for and this base did not have.** `p731` established that spaced repetition has commoditised and that the asset is the *capture surface*, not the scheduler — which is only actionable if the scheduler is available as a component you do not write. A Rust core binds into Python, Node, Swift, Kotlin and WASM, so one scheduler serves a web app, a mobile app and a batch job without three reimplementations of the same algorithm. |
| [openedx/edx-cookiecutters](https://github.com/openedx/edx-cookiecutters) | 30 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-10-28 | Global | **First-party Open edX project templates** for new apps, libraries and services. Gate: `LICENSE.txt`, `11,358 B` raw → **`9942 B`/`3fda30957805`** (stock Apache, and one of the `5` payloads establishing the constant). 🔵 **Why a template repo is filed as a foundation:** Open edX itself is **AGPL-3.0**, which is the single most common blocker on this base's platform shelf. These scaffolds are **Apache-2.0** and they are the sanctioned extension path — so work done at the extension point carries a permissive grant even though the host does not. That distinction decides whether an Open edX engagement is sellable, and `p731`'s `731-E` found the same pattern (`mitodl/open-edx-plugins` is BSD-3-Clause at an extension point measured copyleft elsewhere). |
| [openedx/edx-bootstrap](https://github.com/openedx/edx-bootstrap) | 32 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-09-08 | Global | The **Bootstrap theming substrate for Open edX** — the SCSS layer institutional skinning is built on. Gate: `LICENSE`, `11,356 B` raw → **`9942 B`/`3fda30957805`** (stock Apache; the smallest raw payload in the `5`-payload constant set). 🔵 Filed alongside the cookiecutters for the same reason and as the second half of the same argument: **theming and scaffolding are the two things every Open edX engagement actually touches**, and both are permissive. ⚠️ Lower activity than its sibling; check it still tracks the Open edX release you are targeting. |
| [opencast/helper-scripts](https://github.com/opencast/helper-scripts) | 11 | BSD-2-Clause | 2026-10-30 | **EMEA** | Operational tooling for **Opencast**, the lecture-capture and video-management system run by a large number of European universities — migration, bulk metadata repair and health-check scripts. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,355 B` raw → `1278 B`/`ab132540e375`. ⚠️ **Not certified, and the reason is a finding rather than a doubt:** this is BSD, and `T288` establishes that the gate cannot certify BSD at all. Payload read, bytes recorded, **no stock claim made**. 🔵 **Posture: this is the unglamorous half of a lecture-capture engagement.** Clients with Opencast do not need the capture built; they need migrations, metadata cleanup and monitoring — exactly what this holds — and it is the natural insertion point for transcription and indexing on existing recordings. 🟢 Region placed on the project's European institutional base. |
| [latam-gpt/anonymization-filter](https://github.com/latam-gpt/anonymization-filter) | 1 | MIT | 2025-05-19 | **LATAM** | ⚠️ **Archived, and read this row for the code rather than the project.** Batch **PII anonymisation for large text corpora in Spanish, Portuguese and English**: Microsoft Presidio for general PII plus **custom regular expressions for Latin-American national identity numbers**, as three composable filters (IDs only, PII only, full pipeline). Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,072 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 **Why a `1★` archived repository is filed at all:** it is the **only permissively-licensed artefact in the entire public estate of `latam-gpt`**, the regional-model programme named in UNESCO's cooperation agreement with Chile's CENIA — and the Latin-American ID regex set is the part nobody wants to write twice, because getting it wrong is a data-protection incident rather than a bug. **MIT is irrevocable, so the code is reusable regardless of archive status.** ⚠️ **What it is not:** maintained, a platform, or evidence that LATAM has buildable regional AI infrastructure — `T291` measures that estate exactly and the answer is `0` live permissive repositories. Vendor the filters, own them, and do not take a dependency. |

## Learner-model corpora, classroom instruments and teaching substrates 🆕 new section at `p733`

**`6` rows, all read `2026-11-10`, all gate-passed under `P176`.** This section collects what the
`p733` sweep returned that is **not** an agent and **not** a deployable platform: a national
language standard as a queryable corpus, a physiological measurement rig for classroom use, two
reference curricula that are substrates rather than courseware, and a teaching framework.
⚠️ **Two of the six would have been refused by the gate in force before this pass** — see
`T278`/`T279` — which is why they appear here now rather than at `p730`.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Region | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|---|
| [dhjz/hanzi-study](https://github.com/dhjz/hanzi-study) | 250 | MIT | 2026-09-10 | **APAC** | **Hanzi acquisition for pre-literate children**, built as game levels over three distinct channels — listen-and-identify, stroke-order writing, and pinyin recognition. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,062 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 Region placed on `362` CJK characters. 🔵 **Foundational rather than an app because the asset is the decomposition:** a character-by-stroke-by-phoneme breakdown with difficulty ordering is the substrate any Chinese-literacy tutor needs and almost nobody publishes permissively. The game is a renderer over it. |
| [makebrainwaves/BrainWaves](https://github.com/makebrainwaves/BrainWaves) | 76 | MIT | 2026-11-03 | Global ⚠️ | An **EEG experimentation platform designed for the classroom** — students design, run and analyse their own psychophysiology experiments rather than reading about them. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,086 B` raw → `1042 B` under the prior rule, **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`** under `P176` (`T279` control). 🔵 **The only row in this base that measures a learner's state from a signal rather than from their answers.** Every learner model here — BKT, FSRS, IRT — infers cognitive state from response correctness. This measures it directly, which makes it the one available instrument for *validating* those inferences instead of assuming them. ⚠️ Region unplaced. |
| [Arcadia-1/circuits-and-systems-classroom](https://github.com/Arcadia-1/circuits-and-systems-classroom) | 56 | MIT | 2026-11-03 | Global ⚠️ | **Interactive lessons and reference material for analog and mixed-signal circuits** — ADCs, PLLs, feedback, noise. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,069 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🔵 Filed because of **where the permissive supply is thin**: this base holds a great deal of language, maths and CS material and almost nothing in hardware engineering, which is exactly the vocational tier that `P179` targets and where generated content is least trustworthy without a reference. ⚠️ Region unplaced (the `6` Greek characters are mathematical symbols — `Ω`, `μ` — **not** a provenance signal; a false placement this pass deliberately did not make, per `T281`). |
| [shawkynasr/HSK-official-Query-System](https://github.com/shawkynasr/HSK-official-Query-System) | 48 | MIT | 2026-10-09 | **APAC** (subject) ⚠️ | A query system over **《国际中文教育中文水平等级标准》** — the PRC's official *Chinese Proficiency Grading Standards for International Chinese Language Education*. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,064 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🟢 **Second national-scale standard corpus this base holds**, after `p732`'s exam/textbook tier, and the first that is a *graded competency framework* rather than a question bank: it maps vocabulary, characters and grammar to numbered proficiency levels, which is precisely the external anchor a mastery estimate needs if it is to mean anything to a ministry. 🔵 It is what turns `P174`-style scheduling into **credential-aligned** scheduling. ⚠️ **Region split, stated rather than resolved:** the *subject* is unambiguously a Chinese national standard (APAC); the *publisher* is unplaced — `shawkynasr` has no profile README and an `839`-character repository README carries no country string. Filed APAC on subject, with the publisher left open. |
| [papagiannakis/Elements](https://github.com/papagiannakis/Elements) | 37 | Apache-2.0 | 2026-11-02 | Global ⚠️ | **Project Elements** — a computational entity-component-system in a scene-graph Pythonic framework, built for teaching *neural and geometric computation* as one subject. Gate: `LICENSE`, `11,358 B` raw (stock Apache) → **`9130 B`/`6e1ffb7fc4e2`** (`P176`; `T280`). 🔵 **Foundational because it is a teaching architecture, not a course:** ECS plus scene-graph gives a student a single executable object they extend across an entire term, which is the structure that laboratory-based geometry and graphics curricula need and that notebook-per-topic courseware cannot provide. ⚠️ **Region unplaced, and this is a specific disappointment worth recording:** the publisher is academic and almost certainly European, but a `9,745`-character README carries **no** institution or country string and `users/{owner}` is blocked in this environment (`733-I`). **Left `Global` rather than guessed** — this is exactly the row `T281` would have mis-placed. |
| [kasnerz/animated-llm](https://github.com/kasnerz/animated-llm) | 26 | MIT | 2026-10-12 | Global ⚠️ | A web application that **explains how an LLM works** through built animation (D3.js + GSAP) rather than prose. Gate: `LICENSE`, `1,072 B` raw → **`1020 B`/`fe2a9817987f`**. 🔵 **Filed under a stated exception to `727-C`.** That rule excludes *teaches-AI* material from `agents/top.md`, and this page is where the exception belongs: AI-literacy instruction is now a **statutory curriculum obligation** in several jurisdictions this base tracks (`T213`, and the ECS finding on embedded AI-literacy standards in `733`'s North America leg), so an explainer is no longer general-interest courseware — it is a deliverable against a mandate. 🟡 This also partially addresses the inconsistency `727-C` declared and left open: *teaches-AI* material is foundational supply, not shelf agents. ⚠️ Region unplaced. |

pass, `275` of them under a permissive grant (`63.1 %`), `268` of them novel to this base, and `108`
permissive *and* pushed in `2026`.** This base has recorded the North American institutional
publisher tier as absent or empty across several passes; it is not absent, it is **infrastructure
rather than pedagogy**, which is why every leg phrased around tutors and agents missed it. See
`T267` for the measurement and `T268` for the one estate that inverts the pattern.

Documented, long-maintained (since 2019). 🟢 **New at p724.** |
| [BennettSchwartz/membrane](https://github.com/BennettSchwartz/membrane) | 96 | MIT | 2026-11-05 | A **typed, revisable, decayable** memory substrate for agentic systems, with *competence* learning and trust-aware retrieval. `LICENSE`, `1,073 B`, © *2025 Bennett Schwartz*; normalised grant body **`870 B` / sha256₁₂ `3d87852cf34a`** — stock MIT by the `T258`/`P164` test. 🔴 **Corrected at `p729`: this cell read `1,020 B` / `fe2a9817987f`, citing `p728`'s published constant, which `729-A` has shown no normalisation reproduces.** The row's verdict is unchanged — it is stock MIT either way — but the numbers it cited were not re-derivable, which is the point of printing them. 🔵 **Foundational for a reason the author does not advertise: decay plus a competence estimate is a learner model.** This base's two mastery implementations are `OATutor` (BKT, pedagogically principled, tied to its own React/Firebase app) and nothing reusable as a *service*. `membrane` is that missing piece — a general store whose primitives (per-item strength, decay over time, revision on new evidence, retrieval weighted by trust) are exactly the primitives a tutor needs per learner per skill, with no pedagogy baked in. 🟢 **New at p728.** ⚠️ **`96★`, created 2025, agent-infrastructure framing, no education citation**: it carries no mastery-model validity claim, so pair it with `OATutor`'s BKT for anything that must defend its mastery decisions (`P163`); use it alone only where the state is a convenience, not an assessment.|

Ebisu *estimates* — it answers "how likely is recall now", which is what a tutor needs in order to **decide what to teach next**, and what any mastery claim to a buyer has to rest on. With `OATutor`'s BKT it gives this base two independent, auditable mastery models. 🟢 **`Unlicense`: public domain, no attribution obligation at all** — the most permissive tier on any of these shelves. Grant at `UNLICENSE` (`1,211 B`, canonical body `1,042 B` / `fe0829b13da5`), **a ladder path this base's probe did not hold until `p729`** — the probe was extended to find it (`729-C`). ⚠️ `48★`, stable since 2017, TypeScript; the algorithm is the asset and it is documented in the author's own write-ups. |

This renders the model's behaviour as a curve someone can look at. From the `open-spaced-repetition` organisation — the FSRS reference estate itself. |

## 🔴 The AI-output evaluation layer: the repository that fits the gap cannot be staged

This base has carried a declared gap for several passes — **no open-source evaluator for AI
output quality in an education context.** A repository that fits it exactly was found and
verified this pass, and it still cannot be staged:

| Repo | Stars | Index licence | Payload | Verdict |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [learning-commons-org/evaluators](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/evaluators) | 88 | `NOASSERTION` | **`LICENSE` → `404`; `LICENSE.md` → `404`** | 🔴 **Do not stage.** Evaluates AI output against *trusted educational rubrics* — functionally the missing piece, actively pushed (2026-11-07). But there is **no licence file at HEAD on either path**, so there is no grant to rely on. Usable as a design reference and as evidence the category is forming; not forkable, not client-deliverable. |

🔵 Per `722-F` the doc-path `404` does **not** mean the repository is absent — the index
confirms it alive at 88★. A missing `LICENSE` is the opposite problem from a missing README:
an undocumented library is still usable, whereas **an unlicensed one is not**, however
permissive its listing looks. `NOASSERTION` plus a `404` on every licence path is the
strongest available signal of *no grant at all*, and it is the one case where the correct
engineering answer is to reimplement the idea rather than adopt the code.

The gap therefore **stays open**, but it is now a *licensing* gap rather than a *supply* gap,
which is a different and more tractable problem: the rubric-evaluation pattern is public and
reimplementable under whatever licence Globant needs.

## Curriculum and pedagogy standards 🆕 new section at p725

The layer `T203` named as the chokepoint between a demo and a delivery, and the one this page
had no row for until this pass. Both rows are EMEA-authored, both are tiny, and neither is a
platform — they are **vocabulary and access**.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) | 13 | MIT | 2026-04-22 | MCP server over **all three Skolverket (Swedish National Agency for Education) open APIs**: Läroplan (curriculum), Skolenhetsregistret (school-unit register) and Planned Educations. 🔵 The only artefact in this base that reaches a **ministry's own curriculum data** as tool calls rather than as a scraped PDF — which is what turns "aligned to the national curriculum" from a claim into a query. TypeScript, stdio transport, published in the MCP registry. **Sweden — EMEA.** Also on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md). ⚠️ `13★`, single author, last push `2026-04-22`: pin a commit. |
| [YannHY/scenarisation](https://github.com/YannHY/scenarisation) | 7 | **CC-BY-SA-4.0** | 2026-11-01 | Learning-design application (after the UCL Learning Designer) that encodes the **pedagogical vocabulary** as data: eight competency frameworks — Socle commun, **DigComp 3.0**, **GreenComp**, CRCN, Pix, **Pix IA**, PER romand, Florimont — Bloom's taxonomy, 28 generic scenario templates in eight families, and **the AIAS AI Assessment Scale as a first-class field on every activity**. School-system referentials for France, Switzerland, the United States, the three Belgian communities, England, Wales, Scotland, Northern Ireland, the European Schools, the IB and **ISCED 2011**. PHP. **France/Switzerland — EMEA.** ⚠️ **CC-BY-SA-4.0 is a content licence, not a software grant**: take the framework and AIAS vocabularies as *data* (attribution + share-alike on derivatives of that data) and do not vendor the PHP into a client codebase on the assumption it behaves like MIT. |

## Interoperability clients — xAPI 🆕 new section at p726

`p613` filed the `Apache-2.0` xAPI / cmi5 **conformance** tier (US DoD / ADL). What it did not file
is the **client** tier: the libraries an application actually sends statements with. Both rows below
are published by **Rustici Software**, the vendor that authored SCORM and co-authored xAPI, so the
licence posture on the reference implementations is a planning fact rather than a preference.
⚠️ **Both are stale and both are fine for it** — a conformance client tracks a frozen specification,
so "no push in two years" means the spec did not move, not that the code rotted. Check the spec
version before the commit date.

Payload read this pass (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`). The JVM is where LMS integration work lands in public-sector EMEA and APAC estates. **North America (publisher).** |

## Public-sector data layers 🆕 new section at `p730`

A tutor's hardest unmet dependency is not a model — it is **the jurisdiction's own numbers**:
enrolment, school rolls, district geography, demographics. Without them a pilot cannot be
targeted, sized or evaluated against anything a ministry recognises. There was no section for
this, so one row makes one.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [cortega26/chile-hub](https://github.com/cortega26/chile-hub) | 139 | MIT | 2026-11-09 | Chile's public data — **education**, geography, demography, economy, health and electoral districts — curated, normalised, validated and loadable in one line from **Polars, DuckDB, SQLite or Excel**. `LICENSE`, `1,070 B`, sha256₁₂ `277707dcaa3b`, © *2026 Carlos Ortega*. Region **LATAM**, from the subject matter itself. 🟢 **New at p730, and it is the LATAM row that is not another tutor.** 🔵 **Every LATAM opportunity on the market page is phrased as a deployment into a public system, and none of them can be scoped without the system's own statistics.** This is that join table for one country, already cleaned — which is normally weeks of an engagement's first month. 🔵 **File the pattern, not just the repository:** one of these per target country is the cheapest durable asset a studio can build in the region, and `P167` wires it. ⚠️ Pushed today, single maintainer; **re-validate against the source series before any figure reaches a client deck.** |

Region **EMEA** (Germany; `b310 digital gmbh` in the payload and the README). 🔵 **Foundational because concept mapping is the one assessment format that reads a learner's *structure* rather than their answer** — the artefact an AI can mark for missing or wrong links, which multiple-choice cannot see. Self-hosted and permissive, so the maps stay on the institution's own infrastructure. 🟢 **New at p728.** ⚠️ No AI in it: this is the substrate, and the marking layer is the build.|
| [mizaelsouto/CursoTecnico](https://github.com/mizaelsouto/CursoTecnico) | 34 | MIT | 2026-10-25 | Central repository of **practical lessons, labs and exercises for Brazilian technical IT education** (`ensino técnico`). `LICENSE`, `1,084 B`, canonical body `870 B` / `3d87852cf34a`. Region **LATAM** (Portuguese-primary README; topics `educacao`, `ensino-tecnico`). 🟢 **New at p729.** 🔵 **Why a lesson repository is foundational rather than merely content:** `T256` named vocational/TVET as a separate, permissively-licensed supply tier, and the thing that tier is always missing is **graded practical work in the local curriculum's own language**. This is that corpus for Brazil's `ensino técnico` system — the material an AI layer can be grounded on instead of generating from nothing. ⚠️ `34★`, course-material repository: expect no API and no tests. |

Previously published here as "~800★, MIT"; both figures were wrong. |

**Twenty-two foundational repositories, all verified today**, spanning assessment (5),
learner modelling (3), benchmarks and corpora (6), **curriculum and pedagogy standards (2)**,
**interoperability clients (2, new at p726)**, **lecture capture (3, new at p726)**,
delivery (4) and reference (1) — the section tallies overlap because three repositories are
filed under two headings. 🟢 `p726` adds `glowxq-oj`, `pdf-annotate.js`, `TinCanJava`,
`TinCan.NET`, `opencast/studio`, `opencast/editor` and `egovframe-common-components`:
**`7` rows, all seven from publisher-estate enumeration and none from any mandated leg.** Regional coverage is now named for **North America, EMEA, APAC and
LATAM**; p724's page could name only three. 🟢 `p725` adds `Skolverket-MCP`, `scenarisation`
and `h16nning/skola`.

## Gaps — searched and genuinely missing

Recorded explicitly so a later pass does not read silence as coverage:

- **No permissively licensed open-source Student Information System.** Searched; every
  serious SIS is either proprietary or GPL/AGPL. For a client needing SIS customisation the
  honest answer is Odoo/OpenEduCat (LGPL) or build, not adopt.
- 🟢 **CLOSED at p725 — "no EMEA-authored foundational *building block*".** The gap is
  closed by two rows found this pass and filed above:
  [`isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP`](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) (`13★`,
  **MIT**, Sweden) and [`YannHY/scenarisation`](https://github.com/YannHY/scenarisation)
  (`7★`, CC-BY-SA-4.0, France/Switzerland). 🔵 **Both are small, and both were invisible to
  every leg this bullet was ever based on**: the national-exam vocabulary (`Abitur`,
  `baccalauréat`, `Lernplattform`) does not reach them, and neither does any multi-term
  non-English phrase — see `725-D`. They were found by **single-term** legs (`skola`,
  `enseignant`). The honest summary of EMEA supply is now: **thin, small-n, and shaped as
  standards-and-vocabulary rather than as runnable platforms** — which is a different and more
  useful statement than a nil. Historical note: the narrow nil was re-asserted for five
  consecutive passes and the prior wider claim was withdrawn at p723. 🔴 **But the wider claim this bullet used to make — "EMEA supply in
  this industry is effectively absent from GitHub" — is withdrawn at p723 as false.**
  `Selleo/mentingo` (109★, **MIT**, pushed 2026-11-09) is a platform-scale, AI-native LMS from
  a Polish software house, and this base filed it at **p655** in the roster on
  [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) before re-asserting the nil four
  more times. EMEA supply is **thin and not of the building-block shape this page collects**
  — which is a real and useful finding — but it is not absent, and the five-pass nil was an
  artefact of the search vocabulary and of not re-reading this base's own rosters.
- **No open-source automated essay scoring that is both modern and maintained.** The
  category exists but is archaeological: the top results (`nusnlp/nea` 207★ GPL-3.0,
  `siyuanzhao/automated-essay-grading` 121★ MIT) were last pushed in **2018 and 2019**.
  Anyone needing AES in 2026 is building it on a general LLM, not adopting one of these.

| [`JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum`](https://github.com/JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `7ea5cac8cd6a`, read from the bytes at `master`) | `0` | `master` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: `Spanish`, `Swahili` and `Chinese` appear only as *curriculum targets*, never as origin or deployment). A formal-language curriculum where **every lesson is a GENERATOR, not a dataset** — an episode is a pure function of a seed, the vocabulary is invented per episode, the grammar/ontology/proof-calculus is sampled fresh, and the answer is **computed from the construction rather than annotated by anyone**. 🟢 **Why this is a foundation and not a course: it produces an evaluation set that CANNOT have been trained on, because it did not exist until it was asked for.** That is the single hardest problem in assessing an education agent, and this is the only subject on these shelves that solves it structurally instead of by holding out data. Profile runs from reference, equivalence and discrimination up through recursion, parsing, variable binding and quantification to proof, argument and value inference. 🔵 **Count instability recorded rather than resolved:** the `master` README headlines `180 lessons` (×3) but also says `179 lessons` once in the same file, and the repository description says `170`. Three figures, one subject — the generator design is what the row is for; do not cite a lesson count from this shelf. |
| [`Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit`](https://github.com/Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `313f8cce1ca8`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `0` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: no institutional or locative token in the `main` README). A dependency-free toolkit that turns **binary PASS/FAIL criteria** into a weighted score, a 1–5 rating and evidence-bearing rationales. 🟢 **The design decision that makes it a foundation is that it is JUDGE-AGNOSTIC: the caller decides how each criterion is judged — human, heuristic or LLM-as-judge — and the library does only the weighting, scoring and rating MATH, deterministically.** That separation is exactly what an education engagement needs and almost never gets: the grading *policy* stays auditable and reproducible while the *judgment* stays swappable. Encodes three rules in the data model — criteria binary and checkable, weighting explicit, every judgment carries a rationale. 🟢 **Composes directly with the generation rows on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)**: it is the scoring half that `GyanKosh`'s and `StudyForge`'s teacher-review workflows leave unspecified. Ships CI. |
| [`Asperant/ExamEcho`](https://github.com/Asperant/ExamEcho) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,078 B`, sha256₁₂ `1b2308dec8b4`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `0` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **EMEA** 🟢 (placed on repo-file evidence: the `main` README is **Turkish-primary** — `260` Turkish diacritics and `50` Turkish function/domain tokens across `6,666 B`, with the self-description `AI destekli, backend odaklı sınav üretim motoru`). A **backend-only** exam-generation engine that takes scope text, course notes and an exam format and emits a structured exam **blueprint** plus a question stream. 🟢 **Its stated value proposition is the reason it is a foundation: not generating questions but MANAGING THE RELIABILITY OF GENERATION.** It reduces loose LLM output to strict domain rules, keeps the flow alive on malformed or incomplete output with fallback plus retry, and exposes quality as **SSE metadata** so the pipeline is observable rather than opaque. 🟢 **Self-positioned as an engine core and not a product** (`Public Beta`, `tam ürün değil`), with LMS and assessment-pipeline teams named as the intended consumers — which is precisely the integration posture a studio wants in a dependency. |
| [`SiddhantShukla02/study-buddy`](https://github.com/SiddhantShukla02/study-buddy) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,085 B`, sha256₁₂ `84acd7c3f1e7`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `1` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: no locative token; the hosting provider is not an origin). A self-hosted **MCP server — spec `2025-11-25`, Streamable HTTP, official Python SDK** — exposing spaced-repetition quizzing, note-to-flashcard conversion, revision planning and per-learner progress, with every learner's data separated. 🟢 **Two things make it a foundation rather than a demo.** (1) It is the **most current MCP spec revision on these shelves**, so it is the right reference for wiring education tools into any MCP-speaking harness. (2) It ships an **agentic Alexa+ simulator — an LLM agent that discovers the server's tools over real MCP and decides for itself which to call** — which is a genuine integration *test harness* for tool descriptions, not a scripted demo. 🔵 **Read the public instance carefully:** it runs in offline keyword mode with data reset on restart, and the Bedrock path is opt-in via `.env`. The deployable artefact is the server, not the hosted URL. |
| [`flashlearn-ai/flashlearn`](https://github.com/flashlearn-ai/flashlearn) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,080 B`, sha256₁₂ `514eed6780dc`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `5` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: no locative token in the `main` README). Turns **repository code and documentation into source-attributed study cards**, reviewed in terminal or browser under spaced repetition; installs as `@flashlearnai/cli` and runs `flashlearn generate` / `flashlearn review` from inside the project being learned. 🟢 **Filed because it points at the corporate-learning engagement rather than the school one** — onboarding an engineer onto an unfamiliar codebase is the highest-value education problem a technology studio actually gets paid to solve, and this shelf has been thin on it. 🟢 **Source attribution is the load-bearing feature:** a card that cites the file and commit it came from stays correct as the code moves, and goes stale visibly rather than silently. Uses Git commit attribution, so provenance is derived rather than asserted. |

An **accessible, agent-native learning workspace** over documents. 🟢 **Held for the licence posture and the accessibility framing together:** Apache-2.0 with an express patent grant is the strongest of the permissive options for client work, and accessibility-first is a *procurement* requirement in public-sector education rather than a nice-to-have. 🔵 **At `0★` this is a sketch** — filed as a shape and a grant, not as a dependency to pin. 🔵 **Grant note:** `11,408 B` is Apache-2.0 **with an appendix filled in** (canonical as-served is `11,357`–`11,358 B`); the `51`-byte delta is the copyright line, not a modified clause. |

An academic assistant built on **LoRA/QLoRA fine-tuning plus RAG** rather than on prompting alone. 🟢 **Filed because it is the FINE-TUNING reference point this shelf lacked:** almost every education row here is retrieval-and-prompt, and an engagement that must run a small model on-premises for data-residency reasons needs a worked parameter-efficient example, which is what this is. Delivered as a Jupyter notebook, so it reads as a method rather than a service. 🔵 **This row and the next one are p719's two canonical-Apache controls** — identical payload (`11,357 B` / `c71d239df917`) at two unrelated owners, which is what established the one-byte discrepancy against this shelf's recorded `11,358 B` / `cfc7749b96f6`; see `719-I`. |

Python, modest and legible. |

The threshold is doing work.) **Simulates classroom student reactions from a lesson plan.** 🟢 **Held because it inverts the shelf: every other row helps a learner learn or a teacher produce, and this one TESTS A LESSON PLAN BEFORE A CHILD SEES IT.** That is the missing rehearsal stage in every teacher-facing pipeline on these shelves, and it is the natural consumer of `master-teacher`'s and `GyanKosh`'s output. |
| [`tvcuong1802/agentic-star-learning-resource-accessibility-gap-brief-agent`](https://github.com/tvcuong1802/agentic-star-learning-resource-accessibility-gap-brief-agent) | 🟡 **MIT on the template, BUT THE RUNTIME IS PROPRIETARY** (`LICENSE`, `1,068 B`, sha256₁₂ `be1056662503`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `0` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **APAC** 🟢 (placed on repo-file evidence: the `main` README points the deployment at `developers.fd.agenticstar.tm.softbank.jp` — **SoftBank**, Japan). Checks a school's learning-resource catalogue export against **the school's own closed list of required accessibility descriptors**, marking each record-level descriptor present / missing / inconsistent / not evaluated and writing a brief for a data steward. 🟢 **The method is the asset and it is deliberately un-agentic where it matters: "the checking is done by fixed rules against the school's profile — nothing is retrieved or inferred."** A closed-vocabulary conformance check is the correct instrument for an accessibility audit, where a hallucinated descriptor is worse than a missing one. 🔴 **But read the dependency before planning around it: the README states the template "does not run standalone".** It requires the AGENTIC STAR platform (start-up fails immediately without it) and the `agenticstar-agentcore` framework from PyPI. **A permissive grant over a template whose runtime is a proprietary vendor platform is NOT a permissive dependency** — this is the licence-vs-runtime trap, and it is the first instance of it recorded on this shelf. Take the rule table and the descriptor state machine; do not take the harness. |
| [`haidang024/agentic-star-education-learning-resource-accessibility-review-agent`](https://github.com/haidang024/agentic-star-education-learning-resource-accessibility-review-agent) | 🟡 **MIT on the template, SAME PROPRIETARY-RUNTIME TRAP as the row above** (`LICENSE`, `1,067 B`, sha256₁₂ `b22b7ca45c04`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `0` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **APAC** 🟢 (placed on repo-file evidence: same `developers.fd.agenticstar.tm.softbank.jp` / SoftBank deployment target). The **review** half of the accessibility pair whose **gap-brief** half is the row above — filed together because the pair is the unit of reuse and a pass that held only one of them would leave the workflow truncated. 🔴 **Same dependency, stated in the same words — "does not run standalone", AGENTIC STAR plus `agenticstar-agentcore`.** 🔵 **Recorded as a NAMED ESTATE for the next pass:** two repositories at two unrelated owners publishing templates for one vendor platform under a shared `agentic-star-*` naming convention and a shared `EDU-C2-0xx` identifier scheme implies a catalogue, not a coincidence. Owner expansion on this convention is the cheapest supply lead p719 leaves behind, and it is left deliberately rather than spent here. |

CI, codecov and npm releases all present. 🔵 **The education relevance is NOT generic, and it is the reason this is filed here:** Ebbinghaus decay is the same forgetting curve that the FSRS/Anki retention layer already on this shelf implements for *learners*. **This framework applies it to the AGENT's memory** — so a long-running tutor can forget and reconsolidate on the same principle its scheduler uses on the student. 🔴 **Not education-specific, and filed as substrate only.** |

| [`baidu-baige/LoongSage`](https://github.com/baidu-baige/LoongSage) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, **`11,340 B`**, sha256₁₂ `29350b6501a9`, read from the bytes at `main`) — 🟡 `18 B` off the `11,358 B` canonical this base holds; p705 checked the governing part and **operative sections `1`–`9` are present and verbatim** (`169` non-empty lines), so the divergence is appendix/whitespace, not grant | **599** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p705 — and this row is filed with its limit stated in the first clause: LoongSage is NOT an education project.** It is Baidu's (`baidu-baige`) production-grade **agentic RL post-training framework** for frontier LLMs — Ray for scheduling, **Megatron-Core** as training backend, **SGLang** for rollout/inference, ~`16k` lines, Python 3.12, Docker-published. It earns a foundation row because it is the permissive *substrate* under the one thing this shelf's education models keep needing and almost never get permissively: **post-training**. Concretely relevant features: **zero-intrusion agent access** (an agent only has to call a standard chat-completion interface — multi-turn context concatenation, tool-output recognition and loss masking are the framework's job, not the agent's), **multi-paradigm online distillation** (PG-style, GKD-style and mixed, with TopK / full-vocabulary / JSD KL strategies) and **multi-data-source mixed training** where agents, reward functions, teacher models and sampling hyper-parameters are configured *per data source* with controllable mixing ratios. 🟢 Read in one line: this is how a tutor built on a `4B` model like [`CogEvol/CogEvol-4B`](https://github.com/CogEvol/CogEvol-4B) gets *distilled from* a frontier teacher under a licence Globant can ship — and the per-data-source mixing is what lets one run blend a maths reward, a Socratic-dialogue reward and a curriculum-alignment reward instead of collapsing them into one scalar |
| [`Ta0p1/ResolveLab`](https://github.com/Ta0p1/ResolveLab) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,068 B`, sha256₁₂ `2bbd31572d7f`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2026 Allen Huang”*) | **104** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p705, and like the row above it is not an education project — it is the assessment METHOD this base has been describing in prose for several passes, implemented.** ResolveLab runs tool-using agents against a **real HTTP service that injects faults**, and grades the episode **from server state, the commit log and read versions — never from the agent's claim**. The README's framing is the transferable sentence: an agent *“can produce valid JSON, sound confident and still leave the system wrong.”* Faults are chosen so they **change the right next action**: paginated lookup with near-miss references, `If-Match` conflicts (`412`), `Retry-After` (`429`), a `502` **after** a committed write, permission revoked mid-task (`403`), a concurrent operator finishing the job, already-correct tickets, and instructions injected into ticket text. **Side-effect grading** fails wrong-record writes, redundant commits, missing fresh reads, false success and unjustified escalation **even when the final record looks right**. Evidence discipline is unusually strong: studies are **registered before any model run**, test inputs and weights are **hash-locked before testing**, and every published episode **replays over real HTTP without a GPU** — its own `v0.5`/`v0.4` protocols and results are in-repo. 🟢 Why a foundation for *education*: swap tickets for a marking task and this is **authentic assessment** — grade what the learner (or the marking agent) actually did to the system, not what they asserted — and it is the only permissive harness on this shelf that treats *“the answer looked right”* as a failure mode to be measured |

| [fasiha/ebisu](https://github.com/fasiha/ebisu) | 🟢 **Unlicense / public domain** — `UNLICENSE`, `1,211 B`, sha256₁₂ `7e12e5df4bae`, *"free and unencumbered software released into the public domain"* | **338** | `gh-pages` | 🆕 **New at p686 — the scheduling primitive this KB had an opinion about but no alternative to.** This base cites **FSRS** `10×` on the agent shelf and `11×` here, and has never carried a second model of forgetting. Ebisu is that second model, and it differs in kind rather than in tuning: FSRS fits a trained parameter set to review logs, while Ebisu keeps a **Beta-distributed posterior over recall probability per item** and updates it in closed form, so it returns *calibrated uncertainty* with every prediction and needs **no training corpus at all**. That makes it the one retention primitive on this shelf that is deployable against a cold start — a new course, a new language pair, a client with no review history. Ports exist in JS, Java and Dart. ⚠️ **Two cautions, both measured:** last push `2024-10-02` (`25` months — the staleness is real, the mathematics is not time-sensitive), and its default ref is **`gh-pages`, not `main`** — a tree-reading pipeline that assumes `main`/`master` will silently read nothing here. 🟢 Public domain is *strictly more permissive* than MIT: no attribution clause to carry into a client deliverable. |
