| [jupyterhub/jupyterhub](https://github.com/jupyterhub/jupyterhub) | **BSD-3-Clause** | **8,364** | `main` | 🟢 **New at p655, and on adoption it is the second-largest platform-class row on this page — behind only `edx-platform`, and unlike it, permissive.** Multi-user notebook server: spawns an isolated, authenticated, resource-limited environment per learner, with pluggable auth (LTI included) and pluggable spawners (Docker, Kubernetes). It is the delivery substrate for every CS, data-science and quantitative course that is taught in a notebook, which is most of them. Grant payload read this pass: 1,474 B, three BSD clauses plus the no-endorsement clause, Jupyter Development Team. **Why it has been missing:** this page searched for *education* platforms and JupyterHub describes itself as infrastructure. It is the correct base for technical-curriculum delivery and nothing else on this shelf substitutes for it. |
| [oppia/oppia](https://github.com/oppia/oppia) | **Apache-2.0** | **6,978** | **`develop`** | 🟢 **New at p655, and the second permissive platform-class row this programme has found — after `OpenOLAT` and before this pass there were two.** A structured-lesson platform built on *explorations*: branching, feedback-driven lessons with misconception-specific responses, built for learners with low prior attainment and deployed through NGO and ministry partnerships in India, Sub-Saharan Africa and Brazil. Apache-2.0 payload **11,358 B** (`sha256₁₂ cfc7749b96f6`, the `apache.org` canonical). 🔧 **p674: previously published as `11,357 B`** — short by `1`; see `verticals/solutions.md`. ⚠️ **Default branch is `develop`, not `main`** — the same trap as `kolibri`; resolve it, never hardcode it. **Why it matters commercially:** Apache-2.0 permits a closed derivative, the pedagogy is explicitly low-attainment and low-bandwidth, and the deployment record is in exactly the markets where this KB's LATAM and Sub-Saharan sections describe demand. |
| [jupyter/nbgrader](https://github.com/jupyter/nbgrader) | **BSD-3-Clause** | **1,370** | `main` | 🟢 **New at p655 — and it retracts this page's longest-standing gap: that no permissively-licensed, education-specific grading system exists.** It does. nbgrader assigns, collects, autogrades and manually grades notebook assignments, with per-cell point allocation, a separated release/submit/autograde/feedback workflow, a formgrader UI and an instructor-facing gradebook. Grant payload read this pass: 1,513 B, BSD-3-Clause, Project Jupyter Contributors. Pushed 2026-11-03 — **actively maintained**, with a decade of university production use behind it. ⚠️ **State precisely what it is:** a *deterministic* autograder — tests, not judgement. It does not score prose. `microsoft/LLM-Rubric` (45★, MIT, also on this page) remains the only permissive primitive for *calibrated* judgement, and the honest architecture is both: nbgrader for what is checkable, LLM-Rubric for what is not. See `compose/patterns.md` **P15**. |
| [CAHLR/pyBKT](https://github.com/CAHLR/pyBKT) | **MIT** | 292 | **`master`** | 🟢 **New at p655.** Bayesian Knowledge Tracing and its extensions in Python — fitting, prediction, cross-validation, and the multi-parameter variants (multigs, multilearn, item-order effects). Grant payload 1,131 B, MIT. Same lab as `OATutor` (UC Berkeley CAHLR), which is the point: **this is the mastery estimator that OATutor's tutoring loop sits on, available as a library you can embed without taking the tutor.** `pykt-toolkit` (457★, also here) covers the deep-learning KT models; pyBKT covers the interpretable, small-data, auditable one — and *interpretable* is what a regulator or an academic board asks for. Pushed 2026-11-02, branch is `master`. |
| [Selleo/mentingo](https://github.com/Selleo/mentingo) | **MIT** | 108 | `main` | 🟢 **New at p655. The third permissive platform-class row, and the only one whose AI layer is in the core rather than bolted on.** A multi-tenant, white-label LMS for corporate L&D with a voice-and-chat AI mentor that runs scored role-play. Grant payload 1,062 B, MIT. Stack read from the manifests this pass: NestJS 10 / TypeScript / Drizzle / **PostgreSQL 16 + pgvector** / Redis / BullMQ, front end Remix + React, AI layer **Vercel AI SDK (`ai` 7.0.148, `@ai-sdk/openai` 4.0.8, `@ai-sdk/workflow` 2.0.80) + `langchain` 0.3.34 + pgvector retrieval + LiveKit voice + Langfuse tracing**. 1,397 commits / 32 forks. Maintained by Selleo (Poland) — **EMEA**. **106★ is not adoption and this row says so**; it is filed because it is the only MIT platform in which multi-tenancy, white-labelling and the agent are all core, which is precisely what a Globant-built, client-branded, closed deliverable needs. See `verticals/solutions.md` and `compose/patterns.md` **P14**. |
| [yetanalytics/lrsql](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql) | **Apache-2.0** | 144 | `main` | 🟢 **New at p656, and the row that gives this base an answer to its own evidence problem.** A **Learning Record Store**: the xAPI-native database that records what a learner actually did — every statement, actor, verb, object, result and timestamp — and serves it back for analysis. Clojure; runs on SQLite, PostgreSQL, MySQL/MariaDB. 🔴 **It is the only live, maintained, permissively-licensed LRS that exists.** The historically dominant one (`learninglocker`, 584 ★) is GPL-3.0 and dormant since 2024-05-30; the reference implementation (`adlnet/ADL_LRS`) is Apache-2.0 but ~12 months quiet. Scope this as a single-vendor dependency, because it is one. T10 and T19 say this sector cannot show its results; this is the component that records them. |
| [adlnet/xAPI-Spec](https://github.com/adlnet/xAPI-Spec) | **Apache-2.0** | **955** | `master` | 🟢 **New at p656.** The Experience API specification itself, from ADL (US Department of Defense) — the data model every row in the telemetry layer implements. Apache-2.0, so the spec text and its examples can be redistributed inside client deliverables. 402 forks. ⚠️ Last pushed 2025-07-03, which for a ratified specification is **correct rather than stale** — do not read this row's date the way you read a library's. |
| [tsugiproject/tsugi](https://github.com/tsugiproject/tsugi) | **Apache-2.0** | 377 | `master` | 🟢 **New at p656 — the permissive way into an LMS without forking it.** An LTI 1.3 framework (PHP) carrying `LTI13.php`, Assignment & Grade Services, Names & Role Provisioning and Deep Linking, **with unit tests**. 4,012 commits, 47 authors, 486 commits in 2026. LTI is how a tool launches inside Moodle, Canvas, Brightspace, Open edX, Sakai and OpenOlat alike — one integration, every platform, and **no copyleft inherited from any of them**. 🟡 Contributor base is concentrated (three authors in 2026). |

This is what lets an agent-generated or agent-wrapped course run inside the content player a client already licensed, rather than requiring a new one. |

Payload is byte-exact canonical Apache-2.0 (11,357 B). 129 forks. 🔵 **Newcastle University builds and runs this for its own teaching — it is production university software, not a paper's companion code**, which is why T20's "all of it is a research artefact" is now stated more narrowly. **EMEA** provenance. ⚠️ Vendors `SCORM_API_wrapper.js` v1.1.20121005 in-tree (header intact) — invisible to manifest scanners, see T16. |

Emits statements from browser-side content to any LRS. 🔴 **Pushed 2023-10-17 — three years quiet, `archived: false`.** Filed because the protocol is stable and the code is small enough to vendor and audit; **treat it as a specification implementation to adopt, not a dependency to track.** `adlnet/xAPIWrapper` (Apache-2.0, 224 ★) covers the same ground and is worse — four years quiet — and is deliberately not filed. |

With `jupyterhub` (BSD-3) and `nbgrader` (BSD-3) already shelved, this completes a **BSD-all-the-way-down technical-curriculum stack that launches from inside the client's existing LMS.** Lowest star count on this shelf (73); it is 230 lines of glue doing disproportionate architectural work. |

Built and run by **TUM** (Technical University of Munich) for its own teaching — production university software, not a paper's companion code. **395 forks, 182 open issues, pushed 2026-11-05, latest tag `10.3.1`.** 🟢 **The AI layer is in-tree and configuration-gated**: **Iris**, an LLM virtual tutor that guides with hints and leading questions; **Athena**, which *suggests* feedback for text, modeling and programming exercises; **Hyperion**, AI-assisted exercise authoring built on Spring AI. | [bigdata-ustc/EduCDM](https://github.com/bigdata-ustc/EduCDM) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (11,346 B) | **203** | `main` | 🟢 **New at p657 — the row that gives this page *cognitive diagnosis*, which it has never had.** A model zoo of **Cognitive Diagnosis Models**: classic **IRT**, **MIRT** (multidimensional IRT), **DINA**, and the neural **NeuralCD** family. 66 forks, 5 open issues. ⚠️ **Pushed 2024-10-25 — dormant.** 🔵 **The distinction from knowledge tracing matters commercially:** knowledge tracing asks *"will the learner get the next question right?"*; cognitive diagnosis asks *"which specific skills does this learner have, and how hard is each item?"* — and only the second produces the calibrated item parameters an adaptive test needs. |

This is the cold-start answer, and the honest caveat is that these are public research datasets, not the client's learners. |

This is the only permissive thing that turns a client's existing PDF question bank into those objects. |

Read `catsim` (live) first and treat this as a formula reference. |

| [17zuoye/pyirt](https://github.com/17zuoye/pyirt) | 🟢 **MIT** (1,083 B) | 111 | `master` | 🟢 **New at p657.** IRT estimation built for scale by **17zuoye**, a Chinese K-12 education company — i.e. written against production homework data rather than a textbook. 55 forks, 6 open issues. ⚠️ **Pushed 2023-08-22 — dormant.** The pragmatic alternative to `girth` when the response matrix is large and sparse. |

City University of New York Computational Linguistics. 🔵 **Why it matters for Globant specifically:** `g2p` covers English. A Spanish, Portuguese, Guaraní or Bahasa pronunciation lexicon is what a LATAM or APAC language engagement needs, and this is where it comes from. |

| [learningeconomy/LearnCard](https://github.com/learningeconomy/LearnCard) | 🟢 **MIT** (1,109 B) | 70 | `main` | 🟢 **New at p657 — and, on the evidence of this pass, the *only* permissive way to issue a digital credential.** A lifelong learning passport around five verbs — **Issue, Earn, Store, Share, Map** — plus **LearnCloud**, an API platform for credential interoperability. The standards stack is in the monorepo's own package list, read this pass: `@learncard/vc-plugin`, `vc-api-plugin`, `openid4vc-plugin`, `sd-jwt-vc-plugin`, `didkit-plugin`, `didkey-plugin`, `did-web-plugin`, `chapi-plugin` — i.e. **W3C Verifiable Credentials, VC-API, OpenID4VC, SD-JWT-VC and DIDs**, not a bespoke badge format. 13 forks, 42 open issues, **pushed 2026-11-05.** TypeScript / Nx / Bun. 🟢 **It ships an agent skill of its own** (`npx @learncard/cli skill install`, with documented "use LearnCard from an AI agent" guidance), which makes it directly composable with the agents on `agents/top.md`. ⚠️ **70 ★ and 42 open issues is a single-vendor dependency** — say so in any proposal. Second, **the permissive code is not a self-hostable primitive**: the pipeline is pinned to closed models — Gemini-2.5-Flash for inference, Gemini-2.5-Pro for annotation, Qwen3-Embedding-8B for vectors — so the MIT grant covers the orchestration and none of the intelligence. |

Judge it on the deployment, not the stars. |

| [`RaspberryPiFoundation/picozero`](https://github.com/RaspberryPiFoundation/picozero) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE.md`, `1,063 B`, sha256₁₂ `97e3b0050f9a`, **raw file bytes** read at `main`; holder *“Copyright 2025 Raspberry Pi Foundation”*) | **142** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p714, and it reproduces `p706`'s defect class exactly: a textbook MIT grant that never once says “MIT”.** The payload opens *“Permission is hereby granted, free of charge…”* and runs the full MIT text without the title line, so a detector keyed on the string `MIT License` classifies it `UNCLASSIFIED` while GitHub's own API — keyed on the body — returns `MIT`. **The two instruments disagree because they read different things, and the bytes settle it.** A beginner-friendly Python library for electronics on the Raspberry Pi Pico: the physical-computing substrate under most primary-school CS curricula in the Foundation's estate. |
| [`RaspberryPiFoundation/python-build-hat`](https://github.com/RaspberryPiFoundation/python-build-hat) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,169 B`, sha256₁₂ `521f26e8fdc4`, **raw file bytes** read at `main`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2020-2025 - Raspberry Pi Foundation”*) | **69** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p714.** The Python library for the Build HAT, which drives LEGO Technic motors and sensors from a Raspberry Pi. 🔵 **Filed because it is the bridge between the two things the EMEA leg named in the same pass** — the Foundation's curriculum estate and *LEGO Education*'s K-8 classroom hardware — under a permissive grant, which the LEGO platform itself is not. |
| [`RaspberryPiFoundation/blockly-keyboard-experimentation`](https://github.com/RaspberryPiFoundation/blockly-keyboard-experimentation) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`, sha256₁₂ `cfc7749b96f6`, **raw file bytes** read at `main`) | **16** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p714, and it is an ACCESSIBILITY substrate, which this shelf was thin on.** Keyboard-only navigation for Blockly — the block-based editor already shelved here as [`RaspberryPiFoundation/blockly`](https://github.com/RaspberryPiFoundation/blockly) (`13,604★`). 🟢 **Block-based programming is inherently drag-and-drop and therefore inherently inaccessible to keyboard and screen-reader users; this is the work to fix that at the library level.** For any engagement with a public-sector accessibility obligation — EU Web Accessibility Directive, Section 508 — this is the dependency that makes a Blockly-based product compliant rather than retrofitted. |
| [`Mangaba-ai/mangaba_ai`](https://github.com/Mangaba-ai/mangaba_ai) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,066 B`, sha256₁₂ `83c8db8d8274`, **raw file bytes** read at `master`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2024 Mangaba AI”*) | **208** | `master` | 🆕 **New at p714, and it is the highest-starred LATAM-placed substrate on either shelf.** A professional multi-agent orchestration framework in Python, documented in Portuguese. 🟢 **Region `LATAM` on a measured margin, not a guess:** the `37,611 B` README returns `104` Portuguese function-word hits against `39` Spanish, and *mangaba* is a Brazilian fruit. 🔵 **It is filed to FOUNDATIONS rather than to the agent shelf because it is domain-neutral** — it orchestrates agents, it does not teach — and that is precisely its value for a Brazilian engagement: the orchestration layer is already Portuguese-documented, so only the education layer above it needs building. 🔴 **Default branch is `master`, not `main`.** |
